# Unfilter digest 09 — pregnancy, birth and the first weeks

Read on 2026-11-17: all 434 quotes in voice/reddit-ur13.jsonl, all 347 in voice/reddit-ur23.jsonl, and all 390 in voice/reddit-ur25.jsonl; the three ledgers’ “What these posts add” sections; the build README and the current findings’ structure and conclusions. Scope: this share only. This is research, not copy.

Every count below counts quote records, never people or prevalence. These posts were selected from historical exclusions. Several ids can come from one post or an evolving thread; crossposts and follow-ups are not independent corroboration. Symptoms, diagnoses, medicine effects, risk numbers, legal/insurance rules and allegations remain attributed testimony. Nothing is withheld or softened because of its subject.

The source quote field is the transcription boundary: existing spelling, censorship, escapes, language and any extraction trims are preserved. Fenced passages below reproduce the entire field. No new ellipses are introduced. Each reproduced record has one primary evidence location; cross-pattern references and the Money/Requests indexes point back to it. A few decisive counter-examples are repeated in the outlier section.

## Changes the final rewrite needs to make

- Branch the stage arc: continuation, ending, loss treatment, grief, a subsequent attempt and surviving/losing a twin are not a single uninterrupted route to birth.
- Replace the partner shorthand about mental load with distinct accounts of ordinary inequity, inability to help, betrayal, reproductive coercion, blocked exit and actual support. A task list cannot answer all of them.
- Name the clinical uncertainty behind some repeated questions. Prior loss after a heartbeat, conflicting scan dates, a reported reversed diagnosis and responsibility gaps are different from a generic excess of Googling.
- Show that asking for help, taking medication, having a supportive partner and paying for help can coexist with severe distress. Include what improved as well as what failed.
- Expand personalisation beyond pregnancy number: loss and assault history, confidential care, medication continuity, disability, other care duties, housing and whether a support person can actually be present.
- Keep the commercial limit: these are payments for named care, tests, supplies and services, or requests for specific help. They do not demonstrate willingness to buy a Mamivio pregnancy guide.

## Pattern map

| Pattern | Relationship to current findings | Where it belongs |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Wanting a child, wanting this pregnancy now, continuing it, and feeling happy about it are separate things. | Extends | §§1–2, 6, 9–10; add a continuation/ending branch to the stage arc. |
| 2. Loss and termination are lived pregnancy stages with treatment, recovery, belongings, anniversaries and grief of their own. | Extends | §2 stage arc; §§4–6, 8–10; add a loss/TFMR/neonatal-death and trying-again section. |
| 3. After loss, reassurance does not accumulate: a heartbeat, symptoms, movement or a finished nursery can coexist with fear of another death. | Extends | §§2, 6 and 9; restored waiting/reassurance and fear/self-blame sections; §10 personalisation. |
| 4. An early scan can start another waiting period, and some accounts describe a diagnosis changing before an irreversible procedure. | Extends | §§4–6; care navigation, risk explanations and waiting; §§10 and 16.2. |
| 5. Normal screening is not experienced as a guarantee, and possible fetal findings raise concrete questions about suffering and the capacity to care. | Extends | §§4, 6 and 9; add explicit screening-result/TFMR material to the decision and care sections. |
| 6. Some partners control conception, sex or pregnancy continuation through sabotage, threats and pressure in either direction. | Contradicts | §5 partners; §§6, 9–10 and the §17.7 historical gap; create a distinct reproductive-coercion subsection. |
| 7. Privacy and safe disclosure include records, paternity, custody and confidential care, not only visitors and social posts. | Extends | §§5–6, 8–10; add record privacy, reproductive confidentiality and paternity/custody as explicit needs. |
| 8. Partner support can fail through betrayal, inability, illness or sudden disappearance, and emotional presence is different from buying things. | Extends | §5 partners and other-side voices; §§6, 9–10; support and backup planning. |
| 9. Stopping or delaying psychiatric medication can precede loss of function, while clinicians disagree and access arrives too late. | Extends | §§2, 4–6, 8–10; medication, work, prior history and care navigation. |
| 10. Severe distress can coexist with love, preparation, medication, therapy and support, and some accounts include rage, self-harm or harm to a child. | Extends | §§2, 5–6 and 9–10; prenatal and postpartum mental-health needs. |
| 11. No immediate bond, muted excitement and grief for one's old life are not the same as not loving or not caring for a child. | Extends | §§2–3, 6, 9–10; identity, shame and bonding. |
| 12. A pregnancy described as normal can still make eating, sleeping, walking, work, medication-taking and caring for children impossible. | Confirms and extends | §2 symptoms and attempts at relief; §§4, 6–9 and 10. |
| 13. Knowing whom to call does not solve refused access, responsibility gaps, unread results or monitoring whose meaning is never explained. | Extends | §§4–6, 8–10 and 16.2 care navigation. |
| 14. Birth choices carry previous loss, pain, disability, treatment limits and a wish for emotional certainty as well as a preferred date. | Extends | §4 birth preferences and pain relief; §§6, 9–10. |
| 15. The explanation, pain relief and respect around a procedure can define trauma even when treatment was necessary or the baby is healthy. | Confirms and extends | §4 consent, pressure and actual birth; §5 being believed; §10 preparation limits. |
| 16. The first weeks may involve profound physical incapacity, continuing procedures, separation from the baby and grief for lost time. | Confirms and extends | §2 first weeks; §4 birth/recovery; §§5–6, 9–10. |
| 17. Feeding goals can become pressure and self-blame when pain, hemorrhage, reflux, supply problems or chest dysphoria make them unworkable. | Extends | §2 recovery/feeding/bonding; §§3–6, 8–10. |
| 18. Generic weight targets, pictures and comments can aggravate shame or eating-disorder habits instead of helping a parent eat and function. | Extends | §§2–3, 5–6 and 9; body, identity, exercise and information design. |
| 19. Substance accounts involve quitting, withdrawal, missing coping rituals, unrecognized exposures and fear of punishment or baby removal. | Extends | §§2, 4–6, 9–10; add substances, coping and surveillance explicitly. |
| 20. Another pregnancy must fit the actual children, disabilities and care duties already in the household, sometimes blocking emergency care or birth advocacy. | Extends | §§2, 5, 9–10; older-child plan and situation-specific personalisation. |
| 21. Announcements, visitors and help are shaped by grief, family entitlement and privacy, and boundaries can either be respected or punish the parent. | Extends | §§3, 5–7, 9–10; visitors and relatives; add grief-sensitive disclosure. |
| 22. Money determines access, housing, recovery and the possibility of leaving a relationship, not only what preparation product feels affordable. | Extends | §8 all money subsections; §§5, 9–10, 16.2 and 17.2 work/leave. |
| 23. Parents want different kinds of information, and algorithms, risk language, tools and product labels can create a new problem while trying to solve the first one. | Extends | §§3–4, 6–7, 10–11 and 16.2; information, requests, stories and commercial claims. |

## 1. Wanting a child, wanting this pregnancy now, continuing it, and feeling happy about it are separate things.

**Placement:** §§1–2, 6, 9–10; add a continuation/ending branch to the stage arc.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> This reader is also trying to be believed, retain a say in birth decisions, afford care and remain able to function.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Make pregnancy continuation, termination, changing one's mind and reproductive autonomy explicit. Stable finances, a wanted child, fertility treatment or prior agreements do not settle the present decision. Do not turn abortion regret, abortion relief, or ambivalence into a universal sequence. The IVF account describes grief at success after accepting childlessness; it is a distinct mechanism, not generic first-trimester shock.

**Verbatim evidence:**

But, having a third is something that I always wanted. but also, we now have to buy a new car, rearrange everyone's sleeping arrangements, and shell out $80k for daycare over the next four years.

So, the morning we were able to test, my world was turned upside down when two lines appeared. I cried so hard from grief and felt absolutely no joy. I was already on the bus to a childless existence and had accepted that. But now I am back at the crossroads I so desperately wanted to get off. I am just terrified and uncertain all the time, and I don't know at all if this is still a good idea. I simply really hadn't thought it would work. <a id="quote-pbu13-0044"></a>

```text
My husband told me yesterday that he regrets not asking more questions when we were in the car on the way to the hospital for the transfer. He sensed that I had closed the book on it, wanted to ask about it but didn't. And now we are in the awful situation where we are considering terminating the pregnancy. Because what people don't understand is that during an IVF journey, you also work on loving life together. You work on seeing the beauty in being together, without children. And you learn to accept that they won't come. When you finally find peace and resignation, it is heartbreaking when everything is turned upside down again.
```

We ended up having a slip up the day before I ovulated for the first time post-miscarriage. The second I thought it was a possibility I could be pregnant I was upset again. Then I had a very convincing indent on a FRER at 10 DPO and I was devastated. I ended up getting my period right on time this past Tuesday and I felt so relieved. But now I am staring down another cycle and I’m trying to decide if I want to go for another baby or if it’s time to close this chapter.
```

I found myself thinking: **If this is how he responds to me when I’m pregnant and falling apart, what is this going to be like when we have a newborn?**  
And then I had a thought that scares me:  
**“Should I have an abortion?”**  
I have even thought about having an abortion and telling my husband that I miscarried because I’m so afraid of what continuing this pregnancy means for my life and our marriage.
```

My partner and I are technically married (no one knows) we eloped and we are set to get engaged with the actual ring this fall like have a party and everything with our friends and family , and want to have a fun ceremony with our friends and family after, have our honeymoon in Greece, etc.

This seems to have thrown a complete wrench in everything, just because I can either get an abortion and risk never getting pregnant again or have the baby and have to push everything else out or not have anything at all.

The reason why I say never get pregnant again is because I have severe endo and am prone to UTIS, BV, and yeast infections. I’m talking monthly horrific periods and horrible infections. I’m currently on keflex that I started last week for a UTI. we just found out I was pregnant two days ago so we’re still in shock. I’ve done a ton of research on SA and now I’m just worried if I go through with this- it could cause an infection and therefore leave scar tissue and also running the risk of never getting pregnant again due to my endo getting worse or just in general with low chance of being able to get pregnant in the first place. ## 2. Loss and termination are lived pregnancy stages with treatment, recovery, belongings, anniversaries and grief of their own.

**Placement:** §2 stage arc; §§4–6, 8–10; add a loss/TFMR/neonatal-death and trying-again section.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> The previous pregnancy is the yardstick and the previous birth sets the fear.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Include miscarriage, ectopic pregnancy, stillbirth, termination for fetal findings, neonatal death, surgical or medication management, and living and grieving for one twin while carrying the other. Recovery can continue across repeated treatments; a subsequent pregnancy does not erase the lost child's due date. Keep parents' incompatible language about embryos, babies and grief visible.

**Verbatim evidence:**

<a id="quote-pbu13-0165"></a>
**[pbu13-0165]**

```text
But you can prepare for it by being realistic about where you are, using the correct terminology, and understanding what is happening from a gestational sense. A first trimester miscarriage isn't the loss of a baby. It's either an embryo or a fetus, which did not yet have any chance of being alive outside of the womb. Mourn it, for sure, but call it what it is. It makes it harder on anyone going through miscarriage to act as though you lost a child, and it's also really insulting to anyone who's lost a child to compare a miscarriage to that.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0051"></a>
**[pbu25-0051]**

```text
Can’t believe I’m here typing this but yesterday morning (Oct 12th) my husband and I ended up being able to get into the OB for an ultrasound a couple of days sooner than originally thought (Oct 14th). We found out during the ultrasound yesterday that I was having an ectopic pregnancy. The location was originally thought to be in my left ovarian tube, adjacent to my left ovary. We were sent straight to the ER, and I had my first surgery ever to remove the pregnancy. The surgery was considered emergency surgery, and it was laparoscopic. Once in surgery, they discovered the pregnancy was actually located in my right fallopian tube and the left side “sac” they saw on ultrasound was actually a cyst. They drained the cyst on the left and removed my right fallopian tube. I was confirmed to be seven weeks which aligns with my original calculation of conceiving on August 30th. Thankfully I was not rupturing, and there were no complications or internal bleeding throughout surgery. I am home now after being discharged earlier today. In lots of pain and very sore, but it’s over.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0053"></a>

We know we can focus solely on the original symptoms and colon issue now without constant stress and worry of hurting our child in order to allow me to seek care. It all just feels so bizarre. I feel such emotional whiplash, and I know my husband does too. He has been so incredible through this entire journey so far, and I couldn’t imagine what I would do without him. I know I’m suffering physically right now with my bowel issues and the surgery recovery, but I am just so worried about his mental, because he tries to stay so strong for me, and I want him to feel allowed to be weak. ```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0238"></a>

```text
Due to some rare genetic mutation which was not detected until week 38, i had 8 times the normal amniotic fluid. Had to undergo 3 amniotic reductions in which they lumped out 2.5L of fluid each time. My belly was literally bursting the entire pregnancy. Every second was painful. I could not breathe, could not talk, could not walk. To add to that the doctors knew something was amiss with the baby but could not detect what exactly until week 38- a week before i was induced. So the stress regarding baby's health just added to my situation.
Finally baby boy arrived and we were told that he would leave the hospital in a week or 2. I was still in shock about the mutation (since all the previous tests done at week 10 had come out fine and i did not know that they were not comprhensive).
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0239"></a>
**[pbu25-0239]**

```text
I had had an emergency c section. It was very traumatic. As i was recovering at home, pumping every 3 hours and sending milk to the hospital, i was consumed by the stress of how i would take care of my delicate baby. I was trying my best to prepare myself. At the same time i also realized that my life had completely chamged and i would not even be ablt to go to the grocery store (let alone my home country) for at least the next 5-6 years (since his genetic mutation was pretty severe but would not impact his life span) 
Meanwhile baby's health kept fluctuating- he underwent a series of surgeries including an open heart surgery. His lymphatic system was not working properly too. In the end when he was 45 days old i had to take the difficult decision to put an end to his misery- the doctors were unable to remove the respiratory support and he could never be able to sustain his breath.
I was left shattered. It took years for me to somewhat heal. I had lost myself twice. Once when i thought i lost my normal life when my sweetest baby arrived and second when i lost him- my baby for whom i had fought every second during my pregmancy.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0240"></a>

```text
We thought that the time had come to try again- at least my husband thought so. He said i would always be afraid and i should just go ahead despite the fear. We decided to go through ivf so that we could get pre implantation genetic testing done.
I had 2 rounds of egg retreivals back to back. Over responding both times. Had tonnes of eggs and hardly anything good quality. Also had ovarian hyperstimulation- so was in bad shape after the second reteival. Then we thought of taking a break for a couple of months. After a month we found that i had accidentally become pregnant and it was an ectopic pregnancy. Before i could even wrap my heaf around it i was given shots of mtx- a low dose chemotherapy drug to remove the embryo. It was like a punch in the gut when i was at my lowest. It took 3 long months to clear the pregnancy and another 2 to regain at least my baselevel strength. 
Then we found that i had some polyos and had to undergo hysteroscopy for it. Now we are told that we can go for fet next month. But i am scared. I want to run away. I wanted a child so badly. But the thought of pregnancy and recovery just makes me puke.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0282"></a>

```text
Went in for my follow up two weeks later, only to find out that there was residual still.

OBGYN said I could either wait, or take misoprostol, or do a D&C. Since the residual is relatively small, I chose misoprostol thinking at least I don’t need D&C.

Then after excruciating pain for a day, I thought it’s finally over, only to find myself in the ER two days later with excessive bleeding and crazy leg pain.

I thought I didnt have any tears left. Then at the ER, I lost it again thinking is it ever gonna end? Is this body even mine at this point.

After 6 hours wait and tests, OBGYN came done and suggested that I do a D&C to get it over with. And after a while I finally decided to do it.

Two days past now, my leg pain is still there - doc said it’s sciatica nerve maybe caused by the misoprostol, and I can only pray that they got everything out and it’s over but who knows…

I’ve always know pregnancy is difficult and being a mother is a lot… but this experience has been beyond what I could imagine.

It sucks that it’s my first pregnancy   
It sucks that we didn’t even get to see the baby  
It sucks that now I feel like my body isn’t mine  
It sucks that even that i get pregnant again, i will worry endlessly because of what has happened
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0316"></a>
**[pbu25-0316]**

```text
Fast forward to 8 weeks, and we go in for a repeat ultrasound. Baby A grew some more but no heartbeat was found. My tech was having trouble getting a clear picture - we saw a clear yolk sac and the baby (albeit a little caterpillar), and she kept mentioning that the baby was curled and at an angle. I noted, “I’m not sure I see a heartbeat..” and she quickly said, “yeah, I can’t detect one for baby A- let’s go ahead and see Baby B!” 

Idk if it’s my hormones, the glossing-over, or her quick shift to my other baby, but I’m literally on the bed devastated. My husband looks heartbroken and she continued to measure baby B - very strong, we could see the head and body and 176bpm heart. So much of me wanted to yell - BUT WHAT ABOUT BABY A? 

She printed photos (of both), handed them to me and said she’d let my doctor know. After about 15 minutes, she came back and said we could leave. My doctor hasn’t called (I reached out to my clinic) and while I know that we didn’t see a heartbeat or find one on the ultrasound, I’m still clinging to something … 

We are traveling tomorrow (out of the country) and I guess my body will absorb Baby A? I feel frustrated with my clinic, unsure of my next steps, and so, so sad. I’m holding joy for baby B - I never thought I’d get the chance to be a mom, and here I am holding space for celebration and sorrow all in one.
```

I'm so grateful to be having a baby and I love my little girl more than life already, but my heart aches so bad for my first one I never got to meet or even give a name.

These references reuse the same evidence, not additional witnesses.

## 3. After loss, reassurance does not accumulate: a heartbeat, symptoms, movement or a finished nursery can coexist with fear of another death.

**Placement:** §§2, 6 and 9; restored waiting/reassurance and fear/self-blame sections; §10 personalisation.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Checking, apps, scans, family stories, media and chatbots may soothe or extend a loop; some people seek direct information and others seek less.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Explain the history behind checking rather than treating it only as information overload. Parents have experienced loss after reassuring scans, rising hormones and persistent symptoms; some therefore distrust both feeling sick and feeling better. Previous gestational ages and due dates matter more than a generic week. Anxiety can return late in pregnancy and can be felt by a partner too.

## 4. An early scan can start another waiting period, and some accounts describe a diagnosis changing before an irreversible procedure.

**Placement:** §§4–6; care navigation, risk explanations and waiting; §§10 and 16.2.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Care navigation includes trust and being believed.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Separate unresolved clinical information and reported diagnostic reversals from habitual reassurance seeking. Include dating disagreements, known ovulation/IUI dates, pregnancy of unknown location, clinicians' uncertainty, and who explains a repeat scan. A small set of reported reversals is not evidence of a diagnostic error rate or a reason to distrust all loss diagnoses.

**Verbatim evidence:**

At my appointment, they performed both an abdominal ultrasound and a transvaginal ultrasound. After looking, I was told they couldn’t find a baby and that it was most likely an anembryonic pregnancy (blighted ovum). They explained that I would most likely miscarry within the next few weeks, and because my body didn’t seem to be miscarrying on its own, we even started discussing scheduling a D&C.

To make things feel even more certain, I had hCG blood work done 48 hours apart. My levels only went from about 73,000 to 75,000, and I thought that confirmed what I had already been told.  
I was devastated. I cried for days. I told my family. I had already accepted that this pregnancy wasn’t going to be viable and was mentally preparing myself for a D&C the following week.
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0025"></a>
**[pbu23-0025]**

```text
Within about a minute of starting the ultrasound, the technician found the baby.  
Not only was there a baby, but there was a strong heartbeat.

I completely broke down crying.  
The ultrasound measured the baby at approximately 7 weeks and 4 days, which matched almost exactly with my later ovulation. That’s what has been so difficult for me to process—a baby measuring 7 weeks and 4 days doesn’t develop from “nothing” in just three days. I still don’t know exactly why the first ultrasound didn’t identify the pregnancy, but the experience completely changed the course of my week.
```

## 5. Normal screening is not experienced as a guarantee, and possible fetal findings raise concrete questions about suffering and the capacity to care.

**Placement:** §§4, 6 and 9; add explicit screening-result/TFMR material to the decision and care sections.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> The decision and care experience need their own treatment.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Keep the difference between screening, further investigation and a diagnosis in the testimony. Include the parents who would terminate for findings, those who decline for now, fear of a disabled child without support, their own disability, twin complexity, and the late discovery that early tests were not comprehensive. Include later healthy outcomes without using them to guarantee the current outcome.

**Verbatim evidence:**

```text
I had my anatomy scan yesterday at 21+5, everything was going great, or so I thought until the OB/Sonographer told us they she believes there’s an anomaly with baby girls heart, I’m not 100% on the correct terminology but I think it is persistent left superior vena cava, where she had a 4th blood vessels rather than the norm of 3. 

From my quick research (dr google of course) in isolation this is pretty benign and doesn’t usually need intervention however it is a sign or symptom of the possibility of Down Syndrome. 

We’ve done the NIPT around 11 weeks and the result for Trisomy 21 was low probability & she also doesn’t have any of the physical or soft features of Down Syndrome.

We’ve been referred to a paediatric cardiologist to further investigate her heart, in the mean time, I am spiralling, I can’t stop thinking about what this could possibly mean for our baby. 

I’m usually able to be quite calm and logical in most situations but being pregnant and this being my first baby, all I’ve constantly think about it, Google all of the possibilities and cry my eyes out.

I know there are so many difficult things people deal with in pregnancy and this is by no means a diagnosis but I just can’t stop the spiral. 

Has anyone been one a similar situation, any re-assuring stories?
```

We went In today totally unaware of what they needed to rescan and after 30 minutes I was asked if I would do an internal ultrasound and I said yes and they did that for another 20 minutes. What they were looking for was the csp in the brain being developed and they were having a hard time seeing it again. With that being said the tech got quiet for a while which made us nervous and then she said the doctor was going to come in which instantly sent us into a panic. The doctor comes in and he stresses that it very well just might be they aren’t getting a clear image and based off of the other scans he feels like we shouldn’t have to worry but he went over that we need to come back again in 2 weeks and if they still can’t find it then we will be sent for an MRI which led to him explaining what could happen if there is an absence of csp even after an MRI. this was such a moment for us that we weren’t expecting and even though everything is pointing more towards we will be okay we both couldn’t help the over whelming feeling that we got. We went back to work and when we got home I was folding some of her clothes putting them away and I was trying to hide that I was crying and my husband came over and started crying also which just broke my heart. After we got our feelings out we were able to think more clearly and are back to being optimistic but it was such a crazy feeling experiencing this intense new love for something we have never felt before and how in that moment at the doctors our reality shifted and all the sudden I became so aware about my feelings towards our baby and how I can’t imagine what will happen if we have to receive bad news.
```

```text
7 days just sees like a lot to be measuring behind the other baby by so early in pregnancy. I was prepared for growth issues to start popping up closer to 28 weeks or later, especially because the bigger of the two babies has SUA and another cord anomaly I can’t recall the name of.

I’m pregnant with di/di twins, so this isn’t a matter of them sharing a placenta, just my uterus. I asked the high risk doctor if I should be worried and got what didn’t feel like a helpful response. I was told I’ll need another growth scan in 4 weeks to determine next steps. I can feel both babies move and they had heart rates in the normal range, though the smaller baby had a lower heart rate than the other. I went from one baby (the one with the cord anomaly) being a stillbirth risk due to possible restricted growth to now both babies being at risk and I’m struggling to handle it. 

I’m so grateful I get to be pregnant with twins, but this was very unexpected. While trying to conceive I told myself I wouldn’t be stressed this time because my last pregnancy my son was super healthy, there were no issues at all. Now I’ve told twins brings a risk of preterm labor and birth that can’t be predicted, one baby isn’t growing as fast, and the other has SUA. I don’t know if I should be as scared and stressed as I am. Any advice or positive stories would be great.
```

## 6. Some partners control conception, sex or pregnancy continuation through sabotage, threats and pressure in either direction.

**Placement:** §5 partners; §§6, 9–10 and the §17.7 historical gap; create a distinct reproductive-coercion subsection.

**Contradicts. Current conclusion:**

> From her side, the gap is mental load more than absence:

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** That sentence cannot describe this material. Add contraception sabotage, obstructed postpartum contraception, unwanted or painful sex, deliberate boundary violations, threats to leave if she terminates, repeated pressure to terminate when she wants to continue, and involvement made conditional on marriage or compliance. A short partner task list is not an answer to these relationships. Retain male partners' own pressure and suspicion in their words too.

I will eventually get some money from the sale of a family apartment in my hometown, but not enough to fully secure my future. Even I love my life where I live, moving to my hometow would be better. My mother would emotionally support me, but I also feel guilty about disrupting the peaceful life she finally built for herself. 

Also, I’m afraid father of the baby could make it difficult for me to return home with the baby in the future due to legal custody restrictions. He could be helpful with raising the child in Zagreb, but it would be emotionally draining for me.

As for the relationship with the father, it spectacularly fell apart during the pregnancy. The paradox is that he wants the baby very much and does not want me to “kill the child,” as he likes to say, but at the same time he wants what he calls a “reasonable woman,” and he clearly does not see me that way.

I don’t even want to go into detail of what happened between us, but I no longer feel emotionally safe moving in with him and living under constant criticism, ultimatums, and judgments about my character.

## 7. Privacy and safe disclosure include records, paternity, custody and confidential care, not only visitors and social posts.

**Placement:** §§5–6, 8–10; add record privacy, reproductive confidentiality and paternity/custody as explicit needs.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Visitors: rules written in pregnancy.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Birth and care plans may need to account for an estranged abusive parent receiving EOBs, fear of a healthcare-employed parent learning about abortion, surveillance by a partner, fear of child removal, and uncertainty over who can be excluded from the room. Paternity testing has money, timing, assault and safety dimensions. These are speakers' accounts and fears, not verified access rights, legal rules or test performance.

## 8. Partner support can fail through betrayal, inability, illness or sudden disappearance, and emotional presence is different from buying things.

**Placement:** §5 partners and other-side voices; §§6, 9–10; support and backup planning.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> The partner gap is the largest theme

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Distinguish willingness, physical ability, addiction, mental-health crisis, betrayal, death, employment constraints and emotional safety. A previously reliable support person can become unavailable. Paid provision and completed tasks need not restore trust. Include partners who are frightened, grieving, controlled or assaulted themselves; do not assign all anger or violence to one gender.

**Verbatim evidence:**

<a id="quote-pbu23-0112"></a>
**[pbu23-0112]**

```text
as my mum left at 2am, she had to wake him up and tell him to look after me and i should be the one sleeping. he ignored her. i tried to wake him up so many times because i was exhausted, sat in my own bodily fluids after 850ml blood loss, falling asleep whilst holding my baby. i begged him to wake up at one point bc i was delirious and he argued “there’s no point in us both being sleep deprived” but decided he was the one that deserved the sleep. eventually he took her, i went to the toilet, and as i returned i almost fainted and had a whole room of doctors rush in.
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0113"></a>
**[pbu23-0113]**

```text

```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0114"></a>

While we were waiting for the doctor to come in, we started talking names, and eventually he brought up her last name. I'd mentioned when we were first reconciling that I want to share a last name with the child I will be birthing and taking to doctors appointments. He seemed to let that go at the time, but then when I doubled down and said if we aren't married, I'm giving baby girl my maiden name, he shut down and turned icy. ## 9. Stopping or delaying psychiatric medication can precede loss of function, while clinicians disagree and access arrives too late.

**Placement:** §§2, 4–6, 8–10; medication, work, prior history and care navigation.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Care navigation includes trust and being believed.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Add medication continuity and individualized trade-offs as a concrete preparation concern. Parents describe stopping before conception, abrupt discontinuation, prior bad medication experiences, sedation that impairs work, contradictory OB/psychiatrist advice, and fear about taking or not taking drugs. Referral, treatment and employer accommodations do not mean symptoms are resolved. Preserve improvements and failed attempts as attributed experiences.

Thankfully, my doctor wrote a letter to my employer requesting accommodations, including a reduced workload, additional breaks, and more flexibility with deadlines to help reduce stress during my pregnancy. My company has honestly been amazing and very understanding, especially since I was open with them about having a miscarriage a few years ago.

But even with the accommodations, I’m struggling badly. I cannot focus at all. My brain feels completely fried. I’m exhausted, nauseous, and barely getting a few tasks done each day. This has been going on for about two weeks now, and I’m falling seriously behind. I’m terrified I’m going to get penalized for it.

Does anyone have recommendations for managing severe ADHD during pregnancy without Adderall? I’m honestly so desperate that I’ve considered taking just 5 mg to catch up on work, but I’m terrified to do that. I’ve tried tea/caffeine in the mornings, but either I crash midday or it makes the nausea worse.

I don’t know what to do. I’m really scared of losing my job or my bonus, and I can’t afford for that to happen.
```

<a id="quote-pbu13-0250"></a>
**[pbu13-0250]**

```text
I am worried that it will get worse. And the combo of pregnancy fatigue/nausea/restrictions with a deeper loss of motivation and my ability to find joy in my day to day life is making me completely non-functional. I have to keep working (my job is my health insurance and I'm primary breadwinner) and I can't do it well like this. I've done an absolute fuck ton of talk therapy in my life, and when I've tried to do more in the recent past it just hasn't helped - I know all the cognitive behavioral considerations, I can talk myself through disordered thinking, I do all the positive thinking things, I practice mindfulness - I just can't cognitively overcome the feeling of being enveloped by persistent, overwhelming sadness. I would also usually turn to exercise, but I've had pretty debilitating nausea and dizziness during the pregnancy with even relatively minor exertion. My favorite physical activities are hiking, kayaking, and rock climbing - none of which can be stopped/given up/abandoned midway through if I start to feel ill (and rock climbing is 100% out, due to the falling risk). So I'm struggling quite a bit.
```

I cry for hours every single day, I’m scared to leave the house, and I just feel completely hopeless and overwhelmed. My BDD has gotten so much worse and I constantly have thoughts about not being able to handle being a mom. How am I supposed to take good care of my son when I’m struggling so much myself?  
On top of that, I keep hearing that when the mother is struggling mentally, it can negatively affect the baby, which only makes my guilt even worse. I’m just constantly panicking. My husband is doing everything he can to support me, holding me and comforting me, but nothing seems to make me feel better.

I have researched ways to handle what I need, while not taking my unborn daughter away from my boyfriend, but that isn’t a guarantee that she will live if I’m gone. So I am fighting so hard to stay because it wouldn’t be fair to take her away from her father. I have nothing to offer anyone in my life, and I worry it will be the same with my child.
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0135"></a>
**[pbu23-0135]**

```text
I know that people will tell me to reach out for help, but I have a hard time doing that after all these years. The only thing that can really be offered to me at this point is crisis stabilization… so being in the hospital a week or two, and discharge when I’m not immediately at risk anymore. My psychiatrist can’t offer much else to me, especially if I am honest with her about the way I’m feeling. Her hands would be pretty much tied. My therapist is very helpful just to listen and validate, and help with putting things into perspective and offering skills to lessen symptoms, but I see her for an appointment then I drive back home to be alone again.
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0137"></a>
**[pbu23-0137]**

```text
Currently 26 weeks pregnant I’ve been struggling with anxiety for a long time, but lately it’s been worse than ever. I’m having 2–3 panic attacks a day, and it’s honestly exhausting. I can’t take my anxiety meds during the week because they make me too drowsy, and I need to be able to function at work.

I constantly feel like I can’t breathe, and there’s this overwhelming sense of vulnerability that I can’t shake. I’ve talked to my therapist and even tried local support groups, but it still doesn’t feel like enough right now.

A lot of these episodes get triggered when I start thinking about labor and my fear of hospitals, and it just spirals from there.
```

Since then, I’ve spent a total of 12 weeks on bed rest & pelvic rest, and now I’m back on it again. <a id="quote-pbu25-0087"></a>

```text
i’ve had family and a friend around most days to keep me from harm/my mind beginning to spiral and my anger starting to get too much, as that is obviously very difficult to deal with while looking after a toddler, especially because the depression and suicidal thoughts/physical health issues have stopped me from even getting him out for a walk for weeks and weeks now.

i just do my best and let him out in the garden, we do water play, i take care of all needs except for being a present, involved parent due to my brain currently, and the guilt eats me up. my partner is drowning in it and feels like my carer rather than my partner. i can’t even drive, make a meal, do anything. but there is no help available for someone like me. every time i get assessed by the crisis team, they make remarks about how i look well dressed, and the house looks clean. that is because i have made it that way to avoid judgement and social being concerned, yet still there is judgement!

however, i am much better off than i was, and haven’t had one of my “episodes” in about 2 weeks. during the episodes i start throwing things, i’m full of rage towards myself because i feel like a burden and so guilty for everything, can’t breathe properly and have to dig my nails into my arms to ground myself.

because no one will help me within any specialised services i’ve had to take matters into my own hands and learn coping strategies that i’ve never learned before. i’m doing much better but still unable to get my child outside, or me.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0122"></a>

```text
Thank god I’ve never experienced it but I did have PPA and PPD, and extreme intrusive thoughts (probably some OCD in there as well, and definitely a heavy sprinkling of postpartum rage, mostly aimed at my husband). 

It got so bad at one point I had intrusive thoughts of jumping out a window or of harming my baby. I never ever \*desired\* to harm her, in fact my thoughts were mostly absolutely consumed with how to protect her - but I was so severely sleep deprived an mentally unwell that I think my subconscious knew I wasn’t fit to protect her, and it was almost like my brian was looking for a way to get out of the endless loop of delirium from severe sleep deprivation, the stress of a colicky newborn on top of that, and the extreme isolation and lack of support I felt. 

Important info: my husband, while not perfect, is truly wonderful and does / did everything he could to support me. He is not the problem, nor is a lack of social support. At the end of the day, it just is what it is when baby has severe reflux, screams 75% of the time she’s awake, and only sleeps in 30 min increments. 

Anyway… I’m almost two years Pp now and soooo much better (thanks mostly to Zoloft, plus time and baby is a happy incredible toddler now!). 

But we are planning to get pregnant again in early 2027, and I am terrified of going through such a hard time again PLUS taking care of my sweet toddler.
```

## 10. Severe distress can coexist with love, preparation, medication, therapy and support, and some accounts include rage, self-harm or harm to a child.

**Placement:** §§2, 5–6 and 9–10; prenatal and postpartum mental-health needs.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> The first weeks include incapacity, feeding pressure and delayed connection.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Do not collapse all distress into sleep planning, birth fear, hormones or an absent partner. Retain explicit suicidal thoughts, hallucinations, intrusive images, admitted rough handling and reported violence. Distinguish unwanted thoughts with explicit denial of intent from admitted acts or intent. Some feared scenarios concern a house, work, fidelity or being valued only for feeding. Already asking for help, looking well dressed or keeping a clean home may conceal need.

**Verbatim evidence:**

I’m 20 weeks pregnant and a first time mom. We had our anatomy scan yesterday and it was perfect. Baby even gave us a thumbs up, and my husband was on a high all day. We went to our favorite Tex-Mex, bought some baby clothes, and picked out some potential paint colors for the nursery. We were supposed to go get stuff for a cute “half-baked” photoshoot today and build the crib this weekend. But it was like today he woke up and was a completely different person. I’m still processing some hurtful, awful things he said to me. His dad came over to help him set up a new fan in the nursery and he asked him to take him to the hospital because he wanted to kill himself. He barely said goodbye to me and wouldn’t let me go with them. It took me hours to get an update from the hospital. They told me he said he wanted to stab himself. I cried on the phone with the nurse. I’ve been a mess all day. It feels like this is an awful dream. He’s struggled with his mental health in the past but he’s been doing really well lately. He was unrecognizable today. I can’t stop crying and haven’t eaten a thing, which makes me feel like the worst mother in the world. I feel like I’m in shock. Our pups are crying and looking for him. I don’t know when he’ll come home, but they told me it wouldn’t be for a few days. He is my best friend and I don’t know how to be without him. He scratches my back until I fall asleep every night. I can’t even be in our room without feeling like I’m going to puke, so I’m sleeping on the couch.

I don’t have time to take care of myself. My body feels sore, swollen, and unfamiliar. I feel like I’m grieving my old life. Even on days that should feel happy, there’s this heaviness I can’t shake. I go through the motions—feeding, changing, putting my baby to sleep. I try to pump, but I struggle with that too, and it makes me feel like I’m failing again since I can’t even produce an 1oz of milk.

I feel so isolated. I’m starting to feel resentment. My partner gets breaks from parenting, and I don’t. I’m needed 24/7. I can’t even step out for something small like a coffee or take time for myself without feeling guilty. My baby depends on me completely, and I feel like I can’t step away.

She cries a lot and rarely seems content, and it makes me feel like I’m not a good mom. Lately, I’ve been feeling disconnected—like I’m just going through the motions of caring for her instead of actually feeling that bond.

## 11. No immediate bond, muted excitement and grief for one's old life are not the same as not loving or not caring for a child.

**Placement:** §§2–3, 6, 9–10; identity, shame and bonding.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Focus on her body and life, not the baby's fruit size

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Include identity grief before birth, delayed prenatal or postnatal attachment, care performed without the expected feelings, and relatives demanding a display of excitement. Practical planning can be an expression of joy. A loving marriage or a long fertility journey does not settle these emotions. The fear can be of meeting the newborn and feeling nothing rather than of delivery pain.

<a id="quote-pbu23-0214"></a>

```text

I couldn’t do an abortion. So i decided, of course I’ll go through with the pregnancy! That is my decision, but im just not excited about it.

<a id="quote-pbu23-0244"></a>

<a id="quote-pbu23-0267"></a>
**[pbu23-0267]**

```text
I never had the pregnancy excitement. My besties wanted to throw a baby shower but I refused as I was worried that something is gonna go wrong. My husband suggested getting some professional photos taken but I refused again because of my paranoia. I thought every doctor's appointment was a nightmare waiting to happen.

But now, I am supposed to give birth soon and I don't want to. I am worried that something might go wrong. How can I come out of this mindset. I feel like I am also torturing my husband with this behavior. It seems like everyone around me is excited but me.

<a id="quote-pbu25-0047"></a>
**[pbu25-0047]**

```text
Everything changed almost immediately after I found out I was pregnant. Instead of happiness, I felt panic, sadness and a sense of emotional disconnection that I cannot understand. Since then, I’ve been struggling with depression, anxiety and constant doubts about what I feel for my baby.  
I am already **under psychiatric treatment with Prozac/fluoxetine.** My anxiety has improved somewhat at times, but I still don’t feel like I have my joy or emotions back. That is what scares me so much.  
The hardest part is that **I don’t feel attached to the baby in my womb**. I don’t feel the excitement or emotional connection I imagined I would have. Sometimes I don’t even find other people’s children as cute or moving as I used to, even though children used to affect me very deeply.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0048"></a>
**[pbu25-0048]**

```text
I have even reached the point where I sometimes wish the pregnancy would disappear — **not because I want to hurt myself or do anything to cause a miscarriage, but because I sometimes feel that I cannot tolerate this emotional state anymore and just want it to end.**  
I am **not looking for people to simply tell me that this is normal. I want real experiences.**  
Have any of you wanted your baby very much, but during pregnancy felt almost no happiness, attachment or emotional connection toward the baby?  
Did you ever feel like *“I don’t recognize myself anymore”*, even though you used to absolutely love children?  
Did anyone take antidepressants or receive therapy during pregnancy and initially feel that it wasn’t helping enough, but eventually things changed?  
And most importantly: **Did your feelings change after birth or as your mental health improved?**  
I would especially love to hear from women who were convinced during pregnancy that they would never be able to love their baby, but whose feelings eventually changed.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0061"></a>
**[pbu25-0061]**

```text
Since he was 4 weeks old I had a night nanny 2 days a week and a day nanny 2 days a week. The night nanny has since left but I panic at the thought of having my son for days at a time. His father doesn’t really help me I’m also struggling with being me and not just his mom. I was diagnosed with ppd and 4 weeks pp and I will be honest I go back and forth with taking my meds. Is this ppd or am I not meant to be a mom. I miss doing stuff I like I don’t like having a baby on me all the time and having to get up early and never sleep. Could I feel not connected to my child because of the relationship I have with his father?
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0062"></a>
**[pbu25-0062]**

```text
We’ve been TTC a fourth baby for a year with 3 miscarriages. I conceived my 3 kids quickly at 34, 36 and 38. 

I know how incredibly lucky I am to have my kids. I just love motherhood so dang much. I left a big career to be with them and truly relish every second. It has been the most meaningful part of my life, and I think that’s part of why I keep wanting “one more.”

But I’m starting to wonder if what I’m feeling is truly a desire for another child, or if it’s partly grief over the fact that my fertility is ending/declining and this whole chapter of pregnancy and babies may be closing.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0076"></a>
**[pbu25-0076]**

```text
My husband is completely involved, but he has an office job so he's gone most of the day, even though he helps at night and we take shifts so the other can sleep a couple of hours. Even my mom is helping, but I don't know what to do, I need some glimpse of hope, some sort of light that it gets better. 

I knew that having a newborn was going to be challenging, I knew I was going to be sleep deprived but I hoped for some sort of connection. Not that my baby would be smiling at me from day one, but she rejected breastfeeding, sometimes she hates being held. I know it's not like she's rejecting me, I know I shouldn't take it personally, but I could not feel more disheartened by all of it. Motherhood has broken me in ways I didn't even know were possible.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0080"></a>
**[pbu25-0080]**

```text
I resent my partner for being able to go back to work and see other people, for not having had to suffer constant morning sickness, weight gain, pelvic pain, insomnia, labouring for almost a week before being diagnosed with obstructed labour, having a c section despite wanting a low intervention birth, having a general anaesthetic and surgical complications from the c section, stretch marks, not fitting into clothes anymore, not even recognising myself in photos or the mirror anymore and just thinking “who is that sad, bloated, tired person”?

We wanted this pregnancy so badly. It took us three years, IVF, multiple miscarriages, and all of our savings to get here.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0081"></a>

```text
I know people will tell me to consider if I have PPD/PPA, and yes, I have both. I am on medication, I have started seeing a psychologist, and my partner is aware. I feel like ever since we started TTC, it’s been a constant marathon, with no reprieve.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0134"></a>
**[pbu25-0134]**

```text
My baby is now 9 days old. I started using an electric pump two days ago, but I only get 10–15 ml total from both breasts. A midwife also told me that my nipples are quite large, and my baby cries and refuses to latch in every position I try.

I’ve been told by my midwife that breastfeeding may be very difficult for me.

I know I may need to switch to exclusive formula, but emotionally I can’t let go of breastfeeding. I can’t stop feeling guilty that I wasn’t able to give him the immune benefits of colostrum in the first hours and days after birth, and it makes me feel like a terrible mother.

I wanted a straightforward birth. I wanted immediate skin-to-skin and to breastfeed from the start.

I love my baby and I know he is precious, but I don’t feel a strong bond yet — mostly guilt.

Should I give up on breastfeeding?

And how can I start to feel bonded with him?
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0138"></a>
**[pbu25-0138]**

```text
I find myself grieving the newborn I never had. The experiences we’ve never had. I just wish, for her sake that things were different, and if I’m honest, I sometimes wish she had a mum that wasn’t so petrified all the time. 

I’ve been to the GP twice about this and have been referred to an online therapy course. I’ve done it before, it’s useless. I’m going back to the GP tomorrow and this time I’m going to insist on getting help. Being told that this is a perfectly normal way to feel isn’t going to cut it any more. I mentioned postpartum anxiety, depression and how I think I have PTSD, but it was more or less brushed off as normal.

<a id="quote-pbu25-0165"></a>
**[pbu25-0165]**

```text
For a bit of backstory - I didn't know I was pregnant until about 5 months, and even then I assumed I was in the very early weeks. I had my first scan at 27 weeks. I went into the ultrasound not even knowing if I was keeping the baby (seeing as I thought I was early). I had basically 3-4 months to mentally prepare. This was obviously not planned; I did not think I would ever be able to get pregnant, or have a healthy pregnancy. 

It was terrifying of course. I still feel so young, and I had just gone through so much horror in my life prior to this pregnancy. The bio 'father' told me to abort, every single time it was talked about. I was told the baby would "never have a good life." We broke up shortly after.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0166"></a>
**[pbu25-0166]**

```text

I was super ready to not be pregnant anymore, to "move on" to the better part, but now that it could be any day, I'm dreading it. Right now, I think I would just stay pregnant indefinitely it were (safely) possible. As much as I try not to, I've been crying so much every day. 

My parents and my partner plan to be at the hospital for birth, and I know I won't be alone afterwards, but what if everyone sees that I don't feel anything positive? What if I never do?

<a id="quote-pbu25-0175"></a>
**[pbu25-0175]**

```text
This one time, he asked me whether I felt that overwhelming joy or the sense of motherhood when I first saw the heartbeat. It wasn't like that for me. I told him, motherhood hasn't hit me yet. It's like I'm floating and my conscious is seeing this happen to someone else. And then he looked at me like I was weird. He said he read online that the first time is magical for mothers. And i felt so awful.

<a id="quote-pbu25-0204"></a>
**[pbu25-0204]**

```text
My biggest fear of having children was losing my identity, I am a headstrong career driven woman and compete in competitive figure skating. 

Since finding out I was pregnant I haven’t really felt an overwhelming feeling of “joy” my husband has and he’s ecstatic - I guess it doesn’t feel real to me. My family are over the moon also.

I’m struggling as I feel this loss of identity is starting far earlier than I expected it to. I’m having to give up the majority of sport and what I do have left I’ve got until the end of the first trimester and I need to stop. This sport is my outlet, and I’m just having it taken off me for 9 months. 

I feel like I’m being wrapped in cotton wool. I’ve had to drastically reduce the amount of caffeine I’m drinking, my family are being really sweet around me completing tasks that require physical exertion, my husband is being really protective and this is all lovely but I feel suffocated. 

I burst into tears today because I wanted to do some gardening but couldn’t because I needed bags of compost and I can’t lift them on my own. I’m having to constantly be ‘helped’ doing things and I feel completely useless. On top of feeling exhausted, having extremely sore breasts and starving all the time I feel like my life has been stripped from me over night.

I know that sounds dramatic but it’s honestly how it feels. To a point where I’m not sure if I want to do this anymore and then feel awful for feeling that way.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0205"></a>
**[pbu25-0205]**

```text
My intention is to skate until the end of my first trimester but for some reason it’s making me feel dizzy and my balance is a bit “off” and I’ve developed this subconscious fear of falling which makes sessions unpleasant. Even where I’m trying to have a bit of control e.g., when I stop skating, my body is deciding nope we stop now!
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0247"></a>
**[pbu25-0247]**

```text
After a traumatic miscarriage that was a month of questioning if I was pregnant or not without any bleeding, with a bunch of cells that we never knew if there was a gender or not.   

I am excited, I turn my excitement into planning…and the nursery is mostly done, shower planned, invites ordered, games half put together, clothes thoughtfully purchased for baby and myself.

<a id="quote-pbu25-0258"></a>
**[pbu25-0258]**

```text
My partner is so excited and constantly telling me how much I’m going to make a great mother and that I will be a natural when the baby comes. But I know I’m not great with babies (I’m great with children above 1.5 ish, but babies no!) I honestly don’t find them that cute and am realistic about how much hard work they are. I feel like I’m being set up for failure! Like I’m expected to instantly know what to do with a baby just because it came out of me. 

I feel infantilised all the time as everyone is saying silly things like “now we look at baby” or “how’s baby doing” … I don’t get what’s wrong with “the baby” , hearing people refer to it as “baby” feels silly. 

I feel like my body isn’t my own any more. I don’t recognise myself anymore, I look so fat. I know it’s effecting me as I’m seeing myself slipping back into bad eating habits, like counting calories and going days without eating because it feels like the one thing I can control. 

I feel as though I’ve lost my autonomy, like I’m trapped inside myself and I hate it. 

Whenever I open up about my fears about delivery, or the shame I feel about my own body (I get really nervous about physical exams) I’m told I shouldn’t worry by the midwives because they have “seen it all”. It makes me angry because that misses the point, the point is I haven’t done it ever! And I’m the one in the vulnerable position.
```

additionally i recently moved from pa to fl for a change and so my partner has a chance to be near his family again as i’ve always lived down the street from mine. Please be kind to me as i’m already judging myself for these feelings and having trouble expressing them in a way that makes sense on paper compared to my brain and once again don’t feel like this baby is a mistake or having regrets just really confused by how I am feeling compared to other people I see.

<a id="quote-pbu25-0276"></a>
**[pbu25-0276]**

```text
We’ve been together for 5 years now and I know he is getting ready to propose… before this news broke. I’ve been dreaming of the day he’d propose to me and all of the planning that would be going into our wedding. I love the idea of it being just the two of us, undivided attention as newly weds and getting to cherish the Fiancée phase.

We also just bought a house 3 months ago. Like, it’s still unfurnished. We literally bought this home with the idea of “once we have a kid we’ll start looking for something bigger” not thinking it would happen so soon.

My knee jerk reaction is to terminate. Although we have great jobs and a home of our own, I feel like it’s all so new, like we need to get our footing still. On the other hand, my boyfriend and I know we want kids and I 100% believe we would be great parents. I can’t tell if I’m still in shock or if this sense of calmness is because I’ve always wanted to be a mother. If this happened a year later there wouldn’t  be any question of keeping this pregnancy or not. But the timing of it all seems so wrong.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0296"></a>
**[pbu25-0296]**

```text
recently found out i am pregnant, based on lmp i am about 5-6 weeks along.  i am miserable.  before this, i thought this was something i wanted, but now that it is here i am not so sure.  i knew having a baby would change everything but now that it’s actually happening it is like i can’t accept that.  i am scared.  i am sad.  i want to cry all day long.  i feel like i have made a mistake and am mourning my current life already.

my test lines haven’t been progressing well and i hate that i find myself wishing that this just wouldn’t work out.  i have a husband, but his initial reaction to me being terrified was just “well i don’t understand because i am not scared at all” that’s great?  but i am?  my whole self is going to change?  he just seems to be upset that my mood has been funky since finding out.  i am so envious of the women that experience joy and excitement in this stage.

i know this sort of thing is posted here a lot, but i found myself going crazy not being able to put my feelings into words.  i hate that everyone around me wanted me to get pregnant, so now i feel like i am trapped with no one to talk to about feeling this way.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0362"></a>
**[pbu25-0362]**

```text
Weeks 6-9 is when the symptoms kicked IN. Headaches, constant nausea no matter what I try, gross taste in mouth, fatigue, brain fog, BORED. 

I’m privileged to work a hybrid job. When I go in person, socializing helps to distract, but I feel the symptoms way harder. When I work from home, I doom scroll and lay around to the point where I feel like an actual piece of shit. 

Before pregnancy, I was a VERY busy girl! Working out 3-4 times a week, cooking recipes every night, reading big books, cleaning and on top of responsibilities always, socializing and drinking every weekend, life of the party, major extrovert, and now…. I feel like I can’t do anything to access that fun version of myself. 

I have no “off” switch any more. The days are so long. I feel ugly with no motivation or spark. Everything takes so much energy. I’m just kinda losing my mind. 

Everyone tells me it gets easier in the 2nd trimester but that feels so far away. I don’t wanna be around friends because nobody has kids yet and everyone just wants to stay up late and drink. I feel ugly and have no sex drive. I feel bad for my husband while he has been NOTHING short of understanding, patient, and loving.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0366"></a>
**[pbu25-0366]**

```text
My husband and I unexpectedly found ourselves pregnant with our second child much earlier than we were financially or mentally prepared for. After much back and forth, two no show abortion appointments, we decided to continue this pregnancy. Mainly out of fear of an abortion and that we were messing with fate, and because we had planned to have more children in the future anyways. My first was very much planned and a very loved and happy two year old boy. Will the excitement with this baby come to us in time? All we feel is stress and fear right now. We have our first ultrasound booked and early blood testing showed this is another boy, which is also adding another layer of sadness. Apologize in advance to those who struggle with infertility as I know gender is the least interesting thing about a child and our first little boy is the BEST so I know a second will be too. I just need time to come around to all of it.
```

I’m 20 weeks now, and I’ve been struggling more and more as this pregnancy goes on. For context, I struggled getting pregnant due to endometriosis and pcos, even miscarrying back in January. I feel almost numb towards this baby, and that makes me feel horrible because I wanted this so badly. It’s almost like there’s this disconnect. I don’t hate this baby, but I feel nothing almost, and I don’t know what’s wrong with me. I don’t know how to fully explain it, but I’m so anxious all the time, and it doesn’t even feel like this is my baby almost. I have all this support, and my husband is so supportive but I feel guilty even thinking like this and scared to tell anyone, including him, because I don’t want to seem ungrateful or be a bad mom. I just feel like I should be so happy and I’m not.

## 12. A pregnancy described as normal can still make eating, sleeping, walking, work, medication-taking and caring for children impossible.

**Placement:** §2 symptoms and attempts at relief; §§4, 6–9 and 10.

**Confirms and extends. Current conclusion:**

> These accounts sharpen the earlier complaint about expectations into a problem of daily function; women report benefits as well as failed attempts.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Give concrete capacity and failed-remedy accounts, including breast weight restricting breathing, urinary retention, chronic constipation, broken-bone pain, severe itching, emetophobia and medication intolerance. Ordinary tips can have been exhausted or be inappropriate to longstanding conditions. Nausea can prevent keeping down seizure or thyroid medication and can influence whether a wanted pregnancy continues.

urgent care said it could be a tick bite but they couldn’t rule out that it’s another kind of insect bite either. i live in a city and don’t have any pets so I don’t see how I would’ve gotten a tick bite, but I do live in the northeast where it’s very prevalent so who knows. out of an abundance of caution, the urgent care suggested I take amoxicillin 3x a day for 14 days to wipe out any possible Lyme infection. 

they did also draw my blood to test for Lyme but the results take a week and it has a very high false negative rate. they suggested I shouldn’t wait for additional Lyme symptoms to develop. i ran all this by my OB who agrees I should take the amoxicillin. I really don’t like the idea of taking antibiotics while pregnant but obviously I don’t want to risk Lyme either, especially since it can cause stillbirth and other complications if severe. it just bothers me because I really don’t think I have a tick bite. 

anyway I am starting the amoxicillin today but was looking for reassurance from moms who took it and/or who developed Lyme disease during pregnancy. thanks :)
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0363"></a>
**[pbu25-0363]**

```text
I’m currently 22+2 weeks pregnant, and I’ve been having a lot of abdominal pain since around 16 weeks. I was checked by my doctor several times, and around 18 weeks I started having an extreme amount of Braxton Hicks contractions, pretty much constantly.

My cervix was checked several times and everything looked completely normal. At my 20-week anatomy scan, they decided to check my cervix again just to be safe, and that’s when they discovered that it had shortened to 9.2 mm with funneling.

I was admitted to hospital immediately and started on progesterone and medication to stop the contractions. Unfortunately, nothing seemed to work, and that same evening I lost my mucus plug. I genuinely thought I was going to lose my daughter.  
(They said they couldn’t place a cerclage while I was having so many contractions.)

I was eventually discharged from the hospital, but three days later I started bleeding quite heavily. My cervix still looked the same, and they couldn’t figure out where the bleeding was coming from, so I was admitted again and given medication to stop the bleeding.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0364"></a>
**[pbu25-0364]**

```text
Fast forward to now: I’ve been admitted to hospital three times, but everything still looks more or less the same. I’ll have a day with no bleeding at all, and then it starts again.

The strange thing is that my Braxton Hicks contractions have now completely stopped. I don’t feel them at all anymore, and generally I feel so much better than I did just two weeks ago.

So my question is: why could I still be bleeding? Has anyone else experienced anything similar, especially with a very short cervix, funneling, lots of contractions that then suddenly stopped, and recurrent bleeding with no obvious cause?  
I would really appreciate hearing from anyone who has been through something similar. ❤️
```

## 13. Knowing whom to call does not solve refused access, responsibility gaps, unread results or monitoring whose meaning is never explained.

**Placement:** §§4–6, 8–10 and 16.2 care navigation.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Interpretation: one page per country on who does what and when to call is content nobody in the competitor set sells.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Add the failure modes after a parent has called: old and new OBs redirecting to each other; specialist advice requiring another clinician's request; midwife callbacks taking days; cost, travel, insurance and childcare blocking attendance; portal results before explanations; and uncertainty about whether returning will seem wasteful. Include clinicians who repair this by responding, coordinating and listening.

**Verbatim evidence:**

I left a message with my doctors office asking them to schedule something or call me back and didn’t hear back that day. I suspect I wont hear back until Monday when I’ll be 38 weeks. 

I am extremely stressed about going into labor at home with a breech baby or my water breaking with polyhdramnios at home. By the time my office gets back to me, I’ll already be 38 weeks with nothing on the books about a c section and I’m at my wits end. <a id="quote-pbu25-0070"></a>

```text
The worst part is that I told my OBGYN all of this at my postpartum follow-up, and she brushed it off as normal. I called my primary care office after giving birth, basically begging to be seen, and was told the earliest they could get me in was the end of September.

After a pretty severe panic attack last week, I finally emailed my primary doctor directly and explained everything that had been going on. She responded almost immediately and got me an appointment two days later. I had been waiting for months and struggling so much that, honestly, I expected to be told I still had to wait until the end of the month.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0071"></a>
**[pbu25-0071]**

```text
She prescribed me Zoloft and medication to take when I have panic attacks, and she actually sat with me while I made an appointment with my therapist, who I haven’t been able to see in months.  
It was the first time in nearly four months that I felt genuinely listened to and respected.

So, all of this is to say: keep pushing your doctors.

I’m incredibly grateful that I finally got help today, but the truth is that I’ve been in the worst mental state of my life for nearly four months, and I was repeatedly dismissed by the front office staff at both my OBGYN and primary care office before I finally reached my doctor directly.

I honestly feel a little dumb that I didn’t message my doctor myself sooner. But I also shouldn’t have had to fight this hard to be taken seriously.
```

```text
Decided to get a private early viability scan around the 27th march when I'd have been 7 weeks. They couldn't see a gestational sac in the uterus. A quick sweep of nearby structures didn't pick up an ectopic either. I was told to book back in two weeks for a repeat scan. I was devastated by this but held a glimmer of hope. 

Woke up on the 1st of April with Lower abdominal pain that steadily worsened to excruciating within 2 hours. Took myself to A and E and was seen fairly quickly and sent to gynae ward where I dropped my blood pressure dangerously low and almost lost consciousness - was taken into surgery immediately.

They found a ruptured ectopic on the right with over a litre of blood loss and clots. Had to take out my right fallopian tube.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0347"></a>
**[pbu25-0347]**

```text
Has anyone experienced a retained placenta after a vaginal birth?

My delivery was very quick (about 15 minutes), but my placenta wouldn’t come out and was extremely stuck to my uterus. It had to be manually removed, which was honestly one of the most painful and terrifying experiences of my life. The doctor had to manually remove it in pieces for almost an hour.

The next morning, an ultrasound showed a 3.5 × 3.5 cm piece of placenta still remaining. I was given the option to wait for it to pass naturally, take Misoprostol, or have a D&C. At the time I was in so much pain and completely overwhelmed, so I chose to wait.

I’ve now had two follow-up ultrasounds (about 2 weeks apart and then another 3 weeks later), and it’s still there.

My OB is now recommending Misoprostol since I don’t have heavy bleeding, fever, or signs of infection. I was leaning more towards a D&C cause I want this over with but a D&C isn’t currently an option for me for another couple of months because I don’t have severe symptoms.

I honestly hadn’t researched retained placenta much earlier because I was just trying to survive those first few weeks postpartum. Now that I’ve started reading about it, I’m seeing some really scary stories about heavy bleeding and infection, and I’m feeling extremely nervous.
```

## 14. Birth choices carry previous loss, pain, disability, treatment limits and a wish for emotional certainty as well as a preferred date.

**Placement:** §4 birth preferences and pain relief; §§6, 9–10.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Flexibility alone does not explain a good or difficult birth.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Retain competing induction, VBAC, planned-section, epidural and unmedicated-birth preferences. A date can end escalating anxiety, preserve partner advocacy or coordinate childcare. Assault history, feared examinations, absent analgesia options, blood tests, previous injury and non-obstetric surgery can change what is tolerable. Do not turn either acceptance or refusal of an intervention into the measure of preparation.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**The Conundrum-** Here is where I need your thoughts, experiences, anecdotes, and/or advice. There is very limited studies, articles, or evidence that I can find online that talk about pregnancy after ARS (fundoplication or otherwise). It seems like surgeons fall under 2 camps.

1. Wait until you're done having kids to have the surgery so you don't risk a pregnancy causing your hernia repair to reform and the ARS to fail. Suffer through the GERD.
2. Have the surgery if your GERD is debilitating or your BMI is high enough to make it a quality of life issue.

Each have their merits, and each have their issues.
```

<a id="quote-pbu13-0272"></a>
**[pbu13-0272]**

```text
One of the other issues with ARS surgery is that vomiting becomes difficult, painful, and sometimes nearly impossible. If I have the surgery and suffer from morning sickness or (God forbid) *hyper emesis*, that could put a lot of strain on the surgery cite, as well as bursting blood vessels from the pressure and retching. Despite this, from some of the medical journals I have read the majority of women reported a positive outcome while pregnant post ARS, and only experienced heart burn toward the very end of their pregnancies.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0104"></a>
**[pbu25-0104]**

```text
Then Around 36 W growth scan consulted suggested for a 38 W induction as I’m on both metformin and insulin. I happily said ok as I was already pretty anxious about aging placenta, reduced movements.  
So I went in for 38w, got admitted but our hospital was so busy with labour, they could not start the process on that day. The process began the next day. Unfortunately as a FTM, nothing worked on me , I did everything under the sun - Misoprostol, pessory, exercises, taking stairs what not !   
I was only 1 cm dilated with a very posterior cervix after some 4 days! I was very disappointed as I thought I have a genuine opportunity for a vaginal delivery. However I was very sure not to go into one more round of induction and getting stuck as I was against forceps and vacuum delivery. Would not risk bub with shoulder dystocia as he was already in 90 percentile. So went ahead with C section with lot of nervousness. I got the slot a day later and it’s already 6th day on antenatal.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0105"></a>
**[pbu25-0105]**

```text
Finally the day has come, with some usual drama 😅 with slots, my c section was done with so much calmness and felt why did I think so much for this call!! The consultant team was a delight and midwife made sure I got skin to skin contact! We finally welcomed our healthy baby boy on 8th Sept making me the most happy mother in this universe to a gorgeous little boy ❤️❤️❤️ . I had a nightmare of postnatal post C section but would not add that as this is a small love letter for my journey to see my little bub from that 2 lines in Jan to Here. 

This is for all those mums who have 1000 apprehensions, doubts, fears as me and giving that glimmer that you will be all good at the end and it’s all worth it for sure, you are so strong 💪 more than you ever imagined !!!!
```
