<a id="quote-pbu25-0108"></a>
**[pbu25-0108]**

```text
Anyway, since I mentioned this historical diagnosis at my early appts, doctors have now been offering me antivirals.  I am typically someone who is very proactive about mine and baby's health, but having done some research, I feel like this may be unneccessary for my situ.

It's my understanding baby will already have received protective antibodies from me during pregnancy, and the antiviral is just a prophylactic measure to reduce the chance of an active genital recurrence during labour.  That said HSV1 (and 2) are extremely common, and a lot of women will not even know they have it.  

Therefore, being as we don't have a program where antivirals are given out 'just in case' to all, the underlying risk that it presents for unknowing asymptomatic carriers shedding during birth must be low.

I would like to hear from others who have had an historic diagnosis of genital HSV1 with no recurrence for several years.  If you were planning for a vaginal birth, did you take the antivirals regardless?
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0133"></a>
**[pbu25-0133]**

```text
I gave birth to my baby boy on April 8 at 41+6 after being induced.

He weighed 4240 g (very large), and the delivery ended in a forceps birth. I had a severe postpartum hemorrhage and lost 2.5 liters of blood. Right after he was born, I briefly held him on a towel placed on my chest, but I suddenly felt sick and vomited, so I had to pass him to my husband. After that, my memory is very blurry. When I became more aware, I was surrounded by two doctors and five midwives for about an hour while they stitched me and managed the bleeding. I received 6 units of blood.

I also developed a hematoma at the stitched area. My blood pressure stayed very low and I had a mild fever for two days. The night after he was born, I had to go back into surgery under general anesthesia to have the hematoma removed and be stitched again. So I was stitched twice.

I breastfed twice on the day he was born and once the next morning (I don’t even know if any milk came out). After that, my blood pressure and iron levels were so low that I felt dizzy, in pain, and unable to do skin-to-skin or try breastfeeding.
```

```text
I feel like the experiences was taken from me, something in my chest split that day. I blame myself for not having a V birth, or think if I hadn't had my water broken he wouldn't have got tangled.
```

```text
I only now got the record from my partner, he stored them when we got home the first time as new parents. I only now learnt that I had APH/ PV bleeding in labour - I do recall a high number in ml of blood loss over 500ml which is considered a risk I never knew it correlated to what I just learnt.

I only briefly looked it up and to my understand its a low percentage of women get it all can be fatal or have morbidity rates that require a c section. 

I still blame myself for what I call a failed labour, but it was still in the end a success due to a healthy yet bossy baby boy. I'm not quite sure my understanding of APH to the fullest and if it overrides all the sad and negative feeling I have towards my labour experience.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0290"></a>
**[pbu25-0290]**

```text
Rode it unmedicated till about 4pm when I was begging for the epidural, last time they had checked me I was only 1cm so they really advices against the epidural this early. Well my water broke at 4:30pm and I was 5cm dilated. Then they gave me the epidural at 7pm and I was able to sleep after the world’s longest day. All night long they ran the Pitocin and the max they could and almost had to rush me to a C section cause my baby wasn’t handling it. Got to about 9.5 cm at 5:30am called my mom and she got there to help. Well my cervix was being an ass and had a lip we just couldn’t get him past so the sat me up in thrones position. Well that caused all the epidural to pool into one leg and I was feeling the full force of everything. They let me lay down again after a while and the epidural started spreading again well then baby fully dilated and dropped to 0 position and fully effaced. My husband had just left to get himself food so I was texting frantically. This was about 9, they had me on my back pushing for 2.5 hours and got basically no where. But I suggested since again my epidural had wore off and I’m more comfortable on my side to roll me there and push. Baby came out in a half hour!
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0334"></a>
**[pbu25-0334]**

```text
* I was started on cytotec (I believe I ended up doing 4 rounds of this), and worse, I was hooked up to a magnesium IV drip because of the risk of seizure.
* The magnesium was fine at first, but quickly went south - I felt truly, truly awful. To my core, I felt rotten. I was sick, had a killer headache, was exhausted but couldn't sleep (and when I finally was asleep, the nurses kept having to wake me up for a blood test - I had my blood drawn from the same elbow ditch every 6 hours for four days! - and an hourly blood pressure check). I was also put on a clear liquids diet at this time, which lasted allllllllllll the way until after I delivered, **three days later**. I was also not allowed to shower anymore, or go to the bathroom by myself (fall risk).
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0335"></a>
**[pbu25-0335]**

```text
* Epidural was administered within the hour, and it was great. I was completely numb from the waist down, but it was well worth it. I could sleep! I then slept for most of the afternoon until the doctor checked me and I was 9.5 cm, effaced, and baby was at station!
* I started pushing, and the epidural pretty much immediately failed at that time. I went from feeling basically nothing to feeling it all. This would have been fine, as pushing itself didn't feel too bad, except that I was having unbearable lower back pain with every contraction. When I say unbearable, I mean *unbearable*. Pain was definitely a 10/10. I was so afraid of the pain that I was having trouble pushing.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0384"></a>
**[pbu25-0384]**

```text
I had gotten the epidural and i didn’t feel any pain until my epidural wore off on my one side and i felt every contraction on the left side of my body. Through this process my nurses were not the most helpful and seemed like they hated their job. To get back to it they gave me better medicine to help the pain then i couldnt get anymore. I woke up later on at night and i had an infection from them breaking my water way earlier as well. Fast forward to about 4am i was at 9cm 100% effaced and i was asked by my nurse if i could “hold it in” because the doctor had another emergency c section after being told she was coming in at 4 and so she suggested another doctor which i desperately needed and it took that doctor 2 hours to even come see me. I felt the urge to push but i somehow held it in until the doctor came in and they removed the internal monitor for contractions and i pushed for 2 hours till the primary doctor came in and i got a second degree tear. My son was born with a few cuts on his head from the monitor, a severe cone head which looked like a second head was growing and he had poop on him which i still don’t know if he pooped inside, and a big red bruise on his forehead. He ended up failing the oxygen test i think it was and was diagnosed with Pphn (Persistent Pulmonary Hypertension of the Newborn) and spent a week in the nicu and they took his newborn blanket and hat. Im not sure if me holding him in so long caused the pphn but i feel so bad for him and the neglect from the staff was unacceptable. Im thinking about a lawsuit but im not sure if it’s worth it.
```

## 15. The explanation, pain relief and respect around a procedure can define trauma even when treatment was necessary or the baby is healthy.

**Placement:** §4 consent, pressure and actual birth; §5 being believed; §10 preparation limits.

**Confirms and extends. Current conclusion:**

> trauma can concern consent and treatment even when the outcome looks successful.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Include the mismatch between a promised gentle ECV and its experienced force, changing risk disclosures under exhaustion, pressure after repeated refusal, withheld prescribed pain relief, requests to hold in a baby, unexplained manual procedures after delivery, and birth-review notes that create more confusion. Some parents understand why treatment was needed and still condemn its handling. Researching emergency-section mechanics did not prevent grief.

**Verbatim evidence:**

<a id="quote-pbu13-0117"></a>
**[pbu13-0117]**

```text
The doctor came in and very deadpan explained that they are offering a scan for a pulmonary embolism. The ONLY reason they were offering is because my D-dima was high, and I was breathless, but it's also meant to be during this trimester. Still, they have to offer it to find out. They explained there are two types of scans, a CT scan which has a risk of 13% increase in breast cancer risk in the mum, or a VQ scan which is higher radiation for the baby, but only causes defects in 1 in 285,000 cases. At first I was going for CT but I remembered the history in my family (we have an aggressive cancer gene BCR-1 that causes aggressive and often terminal cancer - two family members died young from breast cancer because of it). We said yes to the VQ scan reluctantly because it sounded lower risk overall, and then the doctor came back again and said we also have to tell you that the VQ scan causes terminal childhood cancer when it does cause cancer. Breast cancer we can treat at least with a mastectomy, but we can't treat this other cancer. I also asked about not getting a scan, and they went into how there was a risk of death as it could be a pulmonary embolism, and they can't rule that out any other way.
```

```text
I remember they put her on my chest and suddenly I felt very weak. I felt the atmosphere in the room change and there was a sudden panic in the room. I fainted and my babygirl was brought to a table and the last thing I remember she was lying there all alone and they were all standing around me trying to get my uterus to contract again. I remember the last thing was asking my boyfriend to go to our little girl because I didn’t want her lying there alone. I had uterine massage and different medications (oxytocin, …). ## 16. The first weeks may involve profound physical incapacity, continuing procedures, separation from the baby and grief for lost time.
```

**Placement:** §2 first weeks; §4 birth/recovery; §§5–6, 9–10.

**Confirms and extends. Current conclusion:**

> Recovery is not just a supplies checklist;

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Include afterbirth pain described as worse than labor, hemorrhage and transfusion, repeat stitching or surgery, retained placenta, inability to hold or care for the baby, newborn admission, and recovery that cannot be protected even with helpers. A partner may be traumatized too. Include quick or better-than-expected recovery after difficult births; complication counts do not determine the parent's verdict.

**Verbatim evidence:**

<a id="quote-pbu25-0336"></a>
**[pbu25-0336]**

```text
* Eventually, the stars aligned, and my medication cocktail started working consistently on the same day that my baby was discharged. We were finally able to go home!! I was in the hospital for 10 days, baby was in the NICU for 3 1/2 days, and my wonderful husband was with us the whole time. I am eternally grateful for the staff at our hospital - they took **such** good care of all of us that when it was time to leave, I cried, because I was so grateful to them.

I write all of this out not to scare anyone who's anxious about giving birth, but instead to highlight that I'm totally fine and feeling mostly recovered less than two weeks out. For those ten days, it seemed like everything that could go wrong was going wrong, that I had complication after complication after complication, and that we would never get out of the hospital. I kept my spirits (relatively) high though, and just took it one step at a time. I had no choice but to endure and push through, and at the end of the day, I was, and am, totally okay.

And if you're worried about giving birth, trust that you'll be totally okay, too! Good news is that postpartum has been ***WAY*** easier than I was expecting it to be.
```

## 17. Feeding goals can become pressure and self-blame when pain, hemorrhage, reflux, supply problems or chest dysphoria make them unworkable.

**Placement:** §2 recovery/feeding/bonding; §§3–6, 8–10.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> The first weeks include incapacity, feeding pressure and delayed connection.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Describe why skin-to-skin or early feeding was physically impossible, how guilt attaches to asking a nurse to take the baby, how infant feeding aversion is experienced as rejection, and how a known wish not to breastfeed can be overridden. Formula may be wanted or necessary while its cost is hard to absorb. An instruction to feed, pump or rest can collide with pain, time and autonomy.

I also struggle with severe OCD, anxiety/ depression, and insomnia. I took Prozac my whole pregnancy and have been medicated for like 10 years. I also take trazodone from time to time for sleeping. I stopped during pregnancy but since my hormones have been all over the place, I decided to start taking here and there for sleep. Anyway this is me ranting about how guilty I feel from starting her on formula from the start even though I know deep down I simply wouldn’t be able to handle breast feeding as a mom and person but at the same time I’m like, I barley even tried I’m already a terrible selfish mom for not giving it a fair shot and quick to just say it’s not for me. Im just all over the place with things and I feel like this sweet poor little girl doesn’t know any better and I already gave her something that people say is “worse” than what your natural body can give them but I literally chose to do it anyway. I just feel awful and rattled with guilt. This is just my rant I know there’s nothing to do but I just want to feel justified.

<a id="quote-pbu25-0298"></a>
**[pbu25-0298]**

```text
There was barely any time to give me an epidural, but they said since my water didn't break they will do it. So I got it.

At about 6pm it was already time to push. But I can't feel anything. They coached me to push, I tried and tried. After a while they want to give me pitocin but baby's heart rate was not stable so they didn't. But he was stuck.  

Due to his heart rate they had to do a vacuum delivery after I pushed for 3 hrs. I got one tear and episotomy.   
I got a horrible rash all over my back from the tape they used for epidural. I couldn't feel urge to pee for 4 weeks (slowly coming back now). I got a prolapse from pushing so long. Episiotomy scar still hurts, after 6 wks.

The baby was fine, just had a hematoma and very bad jaundice. His jaundice was so bad he couldn't breastfeed at first so it affected my supply. So we combo feed now.
```

## 18. Generic weight targets, pictures and comments can aggravate shame or eating-disorder habits instead of helping a parent eat and function.

**Placement:** §§2–3, 5–6 and 9; body, identity, exercise and information design.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Exercise appears as identity, a hoped-for preparation method, a source of guilt and a question about pain or safety.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Add stopping GLP-1 treatment, cessation of purging/fasting/nicotine, eating to manage nausea, pre-existing body or eating concerns, scarring and sexual dysfunction. A large printed weight on scheduling paperwork repeatedly triggers dieting urges; an explicit no-weight-discussion request can be ignored. Neither thin ideal illustrations nor praise for growing a human necessarily answers the concern.

**Verbatim evidence:**

## 19. Substance accounts involve quitting, withdrawal, missing coping rituals, unrecognized exposures and fear of punishment or baby removal.

**Placement:** §§2, 4–6, 9–10; add substances, coping and surveillance explicitly.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Fear and reassurance have a mechanism.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Keep alcohol, cannabis, nicotine, non-prescribed stimulant use and continued use by partners visible. Quitting can remove a means of eating, relaxing or regulating anxiety; one supportive partner quits too. Parents ask about testing, police or child-welfare consequences and fear disclosing use. Rumors about prescribed drugs or epidurals can change birth preferences. These claims and online stories are not independently verified.

## 20. Another pregnancy must fit the actual children, disabilities and care duties already in the household, sometimes blocking emergency care or birth advocacy.

**Placement:** §§2, 5, 9–10; older-child plan and situation-specific personalisation.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Pregnancy number alone remains too thin a selector.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Include disabled parents, partners or siblings, elder care, a nephew's emergency placement, older school-age children, physically impossible crib transfers, no childcare for triage, and conflict between the partner minding a child and advocating at birth. A second baby's longer sleep can worry someone whose first never slept. Care plans need realistic backup, not an assumed village.

**Verbatim evidence:**

```text
I don't really know the point of this. I guess a cry for help and any ideas or thoughts from people who've felt similarly? I feel so helpless and don't know what to do. I feel like my only option is to push through but how can I live like this? My toddler needs me, and I need to keep my job.
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0230"></a>
**[pbu23-0230]**

```text
This pregnancy has been incredibly hard. Early on, I started bleeding and truly thought I was having a miscarriage. It turned out to be a subchorionic hematoma under the gestational sac. I spent almost three months in bed because I was so exhausted and sick. I couldn't eat anything except bagels and sip ice water. I couldn't exercise, and I barely had enough energy to take care of myself, let alone be the mom I wanted to be for my daughter. & not being able to be the mom I want to be kills me.  
In the middle of all of this, my husband decided it was a good idea to get a puppy even though I said no.  
I'm now 15 weeks pregnant, and while my energy is a little better, I'm still drowning. I have a wonderfully feral 2-year-old who tries to ride the puppy like a horse , and a puppy who's still having accidents in the house and waking us up throughout the night.
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0283"></a>
**[pbu23-0283]**

```text
Anyway, if you can't tell, I r*eally* don't want my son to suffer through my labor just cry for me the whole time. As much as I would love to be able to birth naturally I can't help but feel like it may cause labor to take a long time. Which I would prefer if I can labor at home until the last minute but being able to do that or not will completely depend on my health. And I *seriously* don't want to go down the slippery slope of medical intervention leading me to end up opting for a repeat C-section just to speed things along. Recovery from one C-section was bad enough for a lifetime.
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0339"></a>
**[pbu23-0339]**

```text
When I was about 10 weeks, I was laying on my back in bed talking to my husband and she came running into the room and jumped directly on my stomach. I was in a lot of pain for two days and panicked, and I think that is where my issues with her started. She is constantly jumping up on my stomach despite us telling her “no”. I cannot even lay down or sit next to my husband without her whining, climbing on top of me so he has to pay attention to her, or her nipping at my face to get me away from him. We also have a 5 month cat and she will bite at him (some occasions, hold him down with her paws and bite at him) if he is getting ANY attention from my husband. I’m now 21 weeks and she has started growling at me and doesn’t listen to me anymore. She has never acted this way ever and would listen to us both equally
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0340"></a>
**[pbu23-0340]**

```text
I think i struggled so much on determining if i was just being hormonal and pregnant or if a real issue was occurring because anytime we shared the things she was now doing with anyone, it was always “aww she’s excited” or “she can tell change is happening and is nervous” so i thought i was overreacting or being clingy of my husband because i didn’t want her around us.   
Based on the comments and advice i’ve received, i think i need to just put my foot down and give him an ultimatum. I just want her to go live with his mom where she has access to a backyard and can play whenever she wants, who can train her (as MIL has lots of experience and time), and my husband can still visit her whenever he pleases.
```

<a id="quote-pbu23-0343"></a>
**[pbu23-0343]**

```text
Last time my labor went FAST. My closest family lives 45min away, I only have a couple of close friends that like under 30min away, and his family (who will be watching our child) is closer, but they'll need to take care of her so they can't come with me. Should I ask someone to come stay with us until I go into labor? Or hope that he keeps it together and just have a backup plan in case? Would it be disrespectful to confess what's going on to someone close to me without his permission / knowledge? Should he have a say in who knows considering it's his healing journey, and I really do want to respect his / our privacy?
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0303"></a>
**[pbu25-0303]**

```text
This is my second viable pregnancy after two missed miscarriages and two chemical pregnancies. I have a son who is almost two and who is the greatest thing in my life but my pregnancy with him was hard. I had HG and then placenta previa, hemorrhaged  during labour  and then had crippling mental health problems postpartum. I didn’t plan on getting pregnant again so soon, but I reacted badly to birth control and accidentally got pregnant before I was able to try something else. I wasn’t being reckless, we barely ever had sex anymore and I wasn’t supposed to be ovulating that week - and I track my cycles meticulously. 

I have terrible HG again, made worse by the fact the baby is a girl. I’m almost in my third trimester and still have to take 12 pills a day or I am sick and unable to eat. I had an incarcerated uterus at around 14 weeks -  
A super rare condition where my uterus became trapped against my spine and cut off all flow to my bladder. I went to emergency and had 1600 ml drained- my bladder almost ruptured. I then had to have surgery to correct the position of my uterus, a pessary inserted to keep it in the correct position, and had to wear a catheter for 10 days to let my bladder heal. Oh… and then I got a raging UTI from the catheter. 

My mental health is in the toilet. I have a high stress job and my anxiety is getting really bad. My OB has agreed to put me off work at 30 weeks which is great… but I just have to make it.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0309"></a>
**[pbu25-0309]**

```text
I’ve had daily migraines for 1.5 months so bad that my forehead feels like I have a hot frying pan against it, but no fevers. And the nausea and food aversions (waited too long to eat, and threw up only one time, always have had a strong stomach when it came to vomiting) all I can eat is what I’m craving at the time and NOTHING sounds good, and the nausea from being STARVING hits immediately whenever it feels like it. Theres no relief even with zofran and peppermints, constant sugar free hard candies.     
Now, ik these are normal things for some pregnancies I suppose. But like, dude. On a scale from 1-10, first pregnancy, some days the nausea 6/7 out of 10. But this pregnancy, I’m talking 9/10 nausea, 10/10 migraines, 10/10 tired, I work 12 hour night shifts and have a 3 year old, I feel like work is impossible, spending time with my SO is impossible, taking care of myself or my toddler is impossible (feeling so much guilt). I haven’t been able to shower except every 10 days or something.. (Plz don’t judge, obviously I was and don’t stink etc, I have a lot of issues with hygiene related to my depression for the last 15 years, it’s an everyday struggle before pregnancy). It’s a work in progress.     
Anyways, my bio-dad was a twin in the womb but it didn’t make it, and we had our 8 week ultrasound and only seen one baby. But with how intense, extreme, and severe everything is, I feel like this HAS to be twins, but obviously won’t find out til 20 weeks.
```

## 21. Announcements, visitors and help are shaped by grief, family entitlement and privacy, and boundaries can either be respected or punish the parent.

**Placement:** §§3, 5–7, 9–10; visitors and relatives; add grief-sensitive disclosure.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> Visitors and own-mother/in-law pressure are planned in pregnancy.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Include deciding how to tell a grieving friend, whether to tell an older child after loss, disclosure demanded in exchange for financial help, sharing pregnancy publicly against instructions, and grandparents treating access or naming as entitlement. Family can be helpful, absent, abusive, bereaved or overloaded. Some announce immediately because they want support if they lose the pregnancy; this is not failed caution.

## 22. Money determines access, housing, recovery and the possibility of leaving a relationship, not only what preparation product feels affordable.

**Placement:** §8 all money subsections; §§5, 9–10, 16.2 and 17.2 work/leave.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> The money stress is mostly bills, daycare and leave

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Add deposits and eviction, benefit eligibility failures, denied bills after attending the wrong similarly named hospital, costs of paternity/privacy, job loss after a medical emergency, leave income trapping a parent with an abusive partner, and opportunity costs of treatment. Accommodation, disability leave or stopping work can still leave a parent unable to function or remove a useful distraction. No quote establishes willingness to pay for a Mamivio guide.

Also, baby could come at anytime so it's hard to truly plan for that.

```text
Anyways, we've thought to just bring our child to the hospital with us and have him stay in the room until it's time to push and waiting in the hall with a nurse, but I hate the idea of him seeing me go through the most strenuous time and I don't like him just being in a hospital hallway. Also, yes my husband could just stay with him but I want him in the room with me in case something goes wrong (it did with my first and I couldn't really advocate for myself due to all the distress and I was pressured into things I didn't want to do).

I know I'm not the only one who has gone through not having a village surrounding them to watch their children so I'd like any guidance on how you all navigated it.
```

**Further supporting ids:** [pbu13-0322], [pbu23-0129], [pbu23-0154], [pbu23-0211]

## 23. Parents want different kinds of information, and algorithms, risk language, tools and product labels can create a new problem while trying to solve the first one.

**Placement:** §§3–4, 6–7, 10–11 and 16.2; information, requests, stories and commercial claims.

**Extends. Current conclusion:**

> They trust people and stories more than documents.

**What should change / be retained (interpretation):** Qualify that preference: some ask for good and bad outcomes without fluff, others ask for one hopeful story or no miscarriage talk. Keep algorithmic graphic imagery, symptom interpretation, home Doppler dependence, chatbots, AI-written coparent messages, and an expensive fertility vitamin with no clear stop instruction as separate mechanisms. A routine week lookup can trigger fear. More facts or a positive tone is not a single shared solution.

**Verbatim evidence:**

<a id="quote-pbu13-0024"></a>
**[pbu13-0024]**

```text
Entering my (25F) second trimester I had a fear of having an OB examine my intimate zone. I’ve never enjoyed gynecology visits before entering my pregnancy. But with these hormonal changes presenting all sorts of new symptoms like increased discharge, hyperpigmentation, and strong odor I felt severely self-conscious. My hygiene routine includes regular showers and frequent cotton underwear changes yet there was still a persistent odor. I was embarrassed by the sheer thought of my nether regions being on display. It bothered me that I would shower in the morning, take a 30-minute commute to work, and notice the strong odor again when I drop my underwear to pee not even an hour later.
```

<a id="quote-pbu13-0025"></a>
**[pbu13-0025]**

```text
Then I found an in-store probiotic capsule that worked for me. In my case it was AZO Complete Feminine Balance. It is a daily probiotic supplement designed to support vaginal health that can be taken with a meal or just water. I was skeptical it was what I needed because I ate Greek yogurt regularly, but in one week I was already noticing improvement. That’s when I realized vaginal probiotics and probiotics for the gut are so different. 

Now two weeks later, my loins smell neutral or nothing at all. (And I make this statement even with my heightened sense of smell with pregnancy nose!) The natural discharge is still there of course but that’s nothing a wet wipe before my appointment can’t fix. I can go to my cervical exam with so much more confidence knowing I smell good again.
```

```text
Whenever I’m falling asleep, it comes and shocked me wide awake. So I stayed awake, I was tired yet I can’t sleep, I broke down and sobbed real badly from the torture. Every wave lasted awhile, it gave me severe anxiety and trauma. It was the first time I felt so emotional and upset in my pregnancy that I cried real hard, not sure from the pain, torture, regrets, or anxiety. But it felt like living hell.   
I thought to myself what if this is going to happen nightly from now? How do I survive for another 4 months? How do I love my baby if pregnancy comes with this sickening sort of pain that doesn’t allow me to sleep because honestly Im definitely not feeling it last night, only regrets.   
I got severe anxiety now that that feeling will visit me again later at night that I don’t know if I’ll even be able to fall asleep at all.
```

```text
Tell me your experience of heavy duty symptoms. Did it end in loss despite the symptoms or did you go onto have a healthy pregnancy? 

I’m not looking to look for encouragement, I’m wanting to gather information without fluff. I am currently 7+1 with very little symptoms. Often I see posts where people are looking for reassurance and most of the information collected is encouraging. Which is great, don’t get me wrong, but I’d love to extend the question in a broader way. I’d love to hear the good and the not so good ending please.
```

<a id="quote-pbu25-0389"></a>
**[pbu25-0389]**

```text
I was taking this bougie gummy vitamin recommended by my fertility clinic called Luminary “Create.” They really don’t taste great but they’re expensive so I was finishing up the package I had before canceling the subscription and getting something else. Well, I was looking at the ingredients tonight and saw that it has a super high dose of Coq10 in it, 600mg. My mind immediately flashed back to when I was first pregnant with my son and the doctor telling me to discontinue using Coq10. I don’t know why I didn’t think of this sooner. I knew that the gummies had Coq10! Anyway, now I’ve just absolutely freaked myself out. I’m 6+3 today and have been taking them for two weeks since finding out. Feeling so incredibly stupid. Anybody out there do the same and everything end up fine? You’d think these prenatal would have a very clear warning on them to stop taking once pregnant, especially these since the dose is quite high. Everything I’ve read is it might be fine but can’t say with certainty, which is very unsettling. Imagining that I’ve caused a birth defect specifically and the idea that I’m now waiting until the 20 week scan (I know I’m getting ahead of myself) to find out is really scary.
```

[pbu25-0392](#quote-pbu25-0392), [pbu25-0393](#quote-pbu25-0393), [pbu25-0394](#quote-pbu25-0394), [pbu25-0395](#quote-pbu25-0395). These references reuse the same evidence, not additional witnesses.

## Outliers and counter-examples

These counter-examples qualify the direction of the patterns; they do not cancel the difficult accounts. Selected decisive contrary passages are repeated here verbatim; the rest link to their complete fields above.

### Emergency intervention can feel reassuring and recovery can be easier than expected.

Complication lists, surgery, NICU and induction do not determine the parent's overall judgement. These accounts confirm the restored qualification in §§4–6 and contradict any single bad-outcome narrative.

**[pbu13-0097]**

```text
After getting the use of my legs back and showering we went to see him - i walked up the ward and merrily walked in like 'Where's my bean?' Both the ladies on the ward were laughing like 'girl you had a c section this morning, you should be way more exhausted than this' haha. I honestly feel okay though physically right now, and weirdly like happy? My body hurts a bit but when hasn't it the last few days? I think knowing the trauma of the birth part is over and that he's currently in the best place he can be is keeping me like weirdly reassured? Plus everyone here, I can't fault for a second, they have been incredible.
```

**[pbu13-0374]**

```text
My c-section went alright, no complications except for the fact I started having a panic attack after they made the first cut (I didn't feel or see anything, I just freaked out). I felt it coming on and managed to ask for anxiety meds before they continued. They sent someone to get my husband (which idk why he wasn't in the room before they cut me, but it worked out in the end), the anesthesiologist held my hand until he got in there and then got me the meds while I did the whole breathing techniques.
```

**[pbu25-0336]**

```text
* Eventually, the stars aligned, and my medication cocktail started working consistently on the same day that my baby was discharged. We were finally able to go home!! I was in the hospital for 10 days, baby was in the NICU for 3 1/2 days, and my wonderful husband was with us the whole time. I am eternally grateful for the staff at our hospital - they took **such** good care of all of us that when it was time to leave, I cried, because I was so grateful to them.

I write all of this out not to scare anyone who's anxious about giving birth, but instead to highlight that I'm totally fine and feeling mostly recovered less than two weeks out. For those ten days, it seemed like everything that could go wrong was going wrong, that I had complication after complication after complication, and that we would never get out of the hospital. I kept my spirits (relatively) high though, and just took it one step at a time. I had no choice but to endure and push through, and at the end of the day, I was, and am, totally okay.

And if you're worried about giving birth, trust that you'll be totally okay, too! Good news is that postpartum has been ***WAY*** easier than I was expecting it to be.
```

- [pbu13-0096](#quote-pbu13-0096) — Reluctant call about persistent backache leads to hospital monitoring at 33 weeks.
- [pbu13-0097](#quote-pbu13-0097) — After frightening emergency section, feels physically well and reassured by staff and baby's care.
- [pbu13-0098](#quote-pbu13-0098) — Immediate love and early involvement coexist with NICU admission.
- [pbu13-0340](#quote-pbu13-0340) — Returns after dismissed pain; repeat assessment finds infected gallbladder requiring surgery.
- [pbu13-0365](#quote-pbu13-0365) — Books' one-centimeter-per-hour expectation clashes with severe pain and slow dilation.
- [pbu13-0366](#quote-pbu13-0366) — Epidural permits rest and eating; later supported walking feels helpful.
- [pbu13-0367](#quote-pbu13-0367) — Baby's reality only fully sinks in after birth.
- [pbu13-0374](#quote-pbu13-0374) — Panic begins after incision without pain; clinician holds hand and provides medication while fetching husband.
- [pbu23-0010](#quote-pbu23-0010) — Later update reports conflicting neonatal opinions and normal growth; this is the author's account.
- [pbu23-0098](#quote-pbu23-0098) — Attributes improved anxiety to prescribed progesterone; this records the author's experience.
- [pbu23-0207](#quote-pbu23-0207) — Describes clear explanations, consent, anxiety medication and partner presence during surgical management of loss.
- [pbu25-0019](#quote-pbu25-0019) — Easy pregnancy follows serious illness and pressure toward hysterectomy.
- [pbu25-0023](#quote-pbu25-0023) — Follow up describes resolution after catheterization and lasting trauma.
- [pbu25-0030](#quote-pbu25-0030) — Later reply reports spontaneous turn and vaginal birth after planned section.
- [pbu25-0104](#quote-pbu25-0104) — Four days of unsuccessful induction lead to choosing surgical birth.
- [pbu25-0105](#quote-pbu25-0105) — Calm section and skin to skin coexist with difficult postnatal experience.
- [pbu25-0334](#quote-pbu25-0334) — Magnesium treatment and liquid diet make prolonged admission difficult.
- [pbu25-0335](#quote-pbu25-0335) — Initial relief lets author sleep before epidural fails during pushing.
- [pbu25-0336](#quote-pbu25-0336) — Feels mostly recovered and grateful despite many complications and long admission.

### Early preparation and disclosure after loss can be deliberate, happy choices.

These cut against treating guarded purchases, secrecy or continued checking as inevitable after loss.

**[pbu23-0012]**

```text
I’m 18w and have the crib, stroller, high chair, changing table, and dresser organized and washed as I gather items. I mainly thrift and deal shop—I found the stroller new from someone who buys pallets and resells for less expensive. I’m very excited for this stage of my life and have been planning since last year. I created my registry last fall and have been editing, buying, and tweaking off it since last fall also. There’s maybe two “wanted” items over $100 left to buy.
```

**[pbu25-0203]**

```text
I found out I was pregnant yesterday, and I'm gonna tell my family today at Mother's Day dinner. I've had 2 past miscarriages, and I like to tell my family early because if something were to happen their support is super important to my recovery. I'm really hoping this baby sticks, and I'm really looking forward to sharing the news with them! I'm 3w3d, so crazy early.
```

**[pbu25-0237]**

```text
As the title says, I made it to 5 weeks. Feels so small yet so big to me after a year of trying and multiple chemicals. I still tread lightly, but I’ve quit testing every other day, I’ve stopped looking up what my chances of miscarriage are, and feeling myself become more hopeful and excited rather than only anxious and worried. This will be our first. All tips are welcome, I’m clueless about pregnancy and babies!
```

- [pbu13-0029](#quote-pbu13-0029) — Six-week first-time parent buys small items while postponing major nursery purchases.
- [pbu13-0163](#quote-pbu13-0163) — Father takes hospital night shift to let wife sleep.
- [pbu13-0164](#quote-pbu13-0164) — After 31-week stillbirth, exhausted father describes joy with 31-hour-old daughter.
- [pbu13-0242](#quote-pbu13-0242) — Partner keeps registry gifts after loss and continues small purchases in hope of another pregnancy.
- [pbu13-0388](#quote-pbu13-0388) — Father-in-law readily accepts private texting of baby photos instead of social posting.
- [pbu13-0432](#quote-pbu13-0432) — Prompt scan after red spotting shows heartbeat and appropriate growth.
- [pbu13-0433](#quote-pbu13-0433) — Posts reassuring result to counter overwhelmingly heartbreaking search results.
- [pbu23-0012](#quote-pbu23-0012) — After a miscarriage, she prepares early using thrift shops, resellers and a long-running registry.
- [pbu23-0198](#quote-pbu23-0198) — After leaving a harmful relationship, new pregnancy brings joy with a supportive partner.
- [pbu25-0203](#quote-pbu25-0203) — Prior losses motivate very early announcement to secure support.
- [pbu25-0237](#quote-pbu25-0237) — Reaching five weeks allows reduced testing and growing hope.
- [pbu25-0247](#quote-pbu25-0247) — Expresses excitement through planning and resists family demands to perform it.
- [pbu25-0256](#quote-pbu25-0256) — Later anatomy findings reassure while extra monitoring remains emotionally mixed.
- [pbu25-0257](#quote-pbu25-0257) — Focus on known positive information eases prolonged uncertainty.
- [pbu25-0358](#quote-pbu25-0358) — Excited for labor while rejecting influencer induction tips.
- [pbu25-0361](#quote-pbu25-0361) — Reports medication and long stability after traumatic hospitalization.

### Leaving or being single can bring relief, and a supportive partner can change behavior.

These cut against assuming that continuing a relationship is always the support solution or that single parenting is always the most frightening option.

**[pbu23-0345]**

```text
It was very easy in the beginning because I was ignited by this new and profound sense of love for the life growing inside me. I quit cannabis cold turkey. I withstood the horrible withdrawal symptoms that lasted 2- 3 weeks. Bless my husband, he was always supportive and even quit smoking himself to make it easier for me. I insisted that he is free to smoke (away from the house and myself of course) but he insisted that he doesn’t need to and it’s probably best to quit anyway. He was my champion when the withdrawals affected my mood along with my hormones. The poor guy took a double whammy on multiple occasion for those few weeks.
```

**[pbu25-0027]**

```text
Ex partner doesn’t want anything to do with it. Oddly after experiencing a stillbirth with that partner, and the way the relationship crumbled, I’m relieved I found out and he’s not involved. The last pregnancy was sooo hard and especially since he said he would be supportive but only had moments of actually being supportive. And then the grief was sucky, upon breaking up I found out he more than less blamed me for the stillbirth (I think in honestly he something blames himself and then that gets too much so he blames me). In reality I know that neither of us are to blame, but it still sucks to know those thoughts of his.

Long story short, being without someone who was harming my mental health feels freeing.
```

**[pbu25-0028]**

```text
I know single motherhood isn’t easy, and can’t imagine the difficulty going from 1 to 2 kids as a solo mom. At the same time- after experiencing one abusive coparent, and then an unsupportive relationship, I feel like this will be easier than my last two pregnancies.
```

**[pbu25-0322]**

```text
I handed the ring back to him and said I'm not dealing with this anymore. It was a relief honestly. The whole pregnancy has been nonstop intense arguments between us, and I've worried so much about how the stress might be affecting baby. He hasn't seemed the least bit concerned about that, but I'm finally done with it.
```

**[pbu25-0323]**

```text
Anyway, I don't know if this is just a rant or looking for advice, but I can say that I have an incredibly supportive family who I am so grateful for. They've been warning me for years. My dad was so relieved when I canceled the wedding. Everyone was. 

All this to say that everyone's situation is different, but I'm going into mine single. And I'm ok with that. Pregnant or not, alone is ALWAYS better than with the wrong person.
```

### Parents disagree about desired information and about what grief may be called.

Keep the disagreement. One parent rejects loss talk; others explicitly ask for difficult outcomes. A minimizing account of first-trimester loss sits against an author's insistence that this is their baby.

**[pbu13-0165]**

```text
But you can prepare for it by being realistic about where you are, using the correct terminology, and understanding what is happening from a gestational sense. A first trimester miscarriage isn't the loss of a baby. It's either an embryo or a fetus, which did not yet have any chance of being alive outside of the womb. Mourn it, for sure, but call it what it is. It makes it harder on anyone going through miscarriage to act as though you lost a child, and it's also really insulting to anyone who's lost a child to compare a miscarriage to that.
```

**[pbu13-0414]**

```text
We understand that every case is different and that Reddit cannot tell us what will happen with our baby. **We are not looking for medical advice or false reassurance. We're simply desperately trying to understand what is possible.**
```

**[pbu13-0415]**

```text
Even if your story had a difficult outcome, we'd appreciate hearing it. We're trying to gather as many real experiences as possible before making decisions.
```

Please no talk of MC as I’m already an anxious girly lol.

**[pbu25-0367]**

```text
I’d love to openly ask a question to those who’ve had pregnancies end in losses and healthy babies. 

Tell me your experience with having close to no symptoms during 1st trimester. Did you end up miscarrying? Did you go onto have a healthy baby despite lack of symptoms? 

Tell me your experience of heavy duty symptoms. Did it end in loss despite the symptoms or did you go onto have a healthy pregnancy? 

I’m not looking to look for encouragement, I’m wanting to gather information without fluff. I am currently 7+1 with very little symptoms. Often I see posts where people are looking for reassurance and most of the information collected is encouraging. Which is great, don’t get me wrong, but I’d love to extend the question in a broader way. I’d love to hear the good and the not so good ending please.
```

### The other-side evidence includes a willing caregiver's physical limits and allegations against a pregnant partner.

A damaged hand, stated epilepsy restrictions, psychiatric hospitalization, and reported violence or control by a pregnant partner are distinct from refusing to help. The speaker's characterizations and suspected diagnoses remain attributed.

**[pbu23-0281]**

```text
I’m 20 weeks pregnant and a first time mom. We had our anatomy scan yesterday and it was perfect. Baby even gave us a thumbs up, and my husband was on a high all day. We went to our favorite Tex-Mex, bought some baby clothes, and picked out some potential paint colors for the nursery. We were supposed to go get stuff for a cute “half-baked” photoshoot today and build the crib this weekend. But it was like today he woke up and was a completely different person. I’m still processing some hurtful, awful things he said to me. His dad came over to help him set up a new fan in the nursery and he asked him to take him to the hospital because he wanted to kill himself. He barely said goodbye to me and wouldn’t let me go with them. It took me hours to get an update from the hospital. They told me he said he wanted to stab himself. I cried on the phone with the nurse. I’ve been a mess all day. It feels like this is an awful dream. He’s struggled with his mental health in the past but he’s been doing really well lately. He was unrecognizable today. I can’t stop crying and haven’t eaten a thing, which makes me feel like the worst mother in the world. I feel like I’m in shock. Our pups are crying and looking for him. I don’t know when he’ll come home, but they told me it wouldn’t be for a few days. He is my best friend and I don’t know how to be without him. He scratches my back until I fall asleep every night. I can’t even be in our room without feeling like I’m going to puke, so I’m sleeping on the couch.
```

```text
There has been a few occasions of physical violence from her which I forgave and let go, she has CPTSD from a horrible childhood into young adulthood has had two past partners commit suicide, and believes she does have BPD although I know the symptoms are very similar to CPTSD. She was going to therapy for herself but stopped out of fear. She’s going through prenatal depression as well and I’m terrified of post partum due to what is going on currently.
```

## What fits nowhere

### The stage arc needs branches for endings and starting again.

No current main section treats miscarriage, ectopic treatment, stillbirth, TFMR, neonatal death or trying again as stages in their own right. §§2, 4, 6 and 9 can receive some material, but an always-forward pregnancy-to-birth arc cannot contain these accounts.

- [pbu13-0126](#quote-pbu13-0126) — After surgeries, ectopic pregnancy and IVF, asks whether to continue, pause or change doctors.
- [pbu13-0264](#quote-pbu13-0264) — Describes TFMR at 23 weeks as a loving decision to prevent suffering.
- [pbu13-0265](#quote-pbu13-0265) — Helping excited three-year-old understand baby will not come home adds another layer of grief.
- [pbu13-0355](#quote-pbu13-0355) — Wants baby's sex after D&C and asks whether SneakPeek could still provide it.
- [pbu13-0412](#quote-pbu13-0412) — D&C physical recovery feels easier than sadness and uncertainty about trying again.
- [pbu25-0190](#quote-pbu25-0190) — Loss diagnosis meets minimizing family and absent partner.
- [pbu25-0191](#quote-pbu25-0191) — Baby purchases, due date plans and deductible become reminders of loss.
- [pbu25-0238](#quote-pbu25-0238) — Severe symptoms and late diagnosis followed apparently reassuring early tests.
- [pbu25-0239](#quote-pbu25-0239) — Pumping during surgical recovery preceded infant death and compounded loss.
- [pbu25-0240](#quote-pbu25-0240) — Further procedures and ectopic pregnancy intensify fear of trying again.
- [pbu25-0282](#quote-pbu25-0282) — Escalating miscarriage treatments prolong loss and fear of future pregnancy.
- [pbu25-0316](#quote-pbu25-0316) — Twin loss is glossed over while another heartbeat leaves joy and sorrow together.
- [pbu25-0325](#quote-pbu25-0325) — Building crib near lost babys due date unexpectedly reopens grief.
- [pbu25-0355](#quote-pbu25-0355) — Seventeen week loss makes future pregnancy frightening.

### Research voices include pregnant minors and confidentiality needs.

§9 says “No minors were quoted as target customers.” These rereads contain a pregnant 16-year-old, a teen asking to exclude her mother from birth, and a 17-year-old planning an announcement. Record their words without making a customer-targeting inference. Reproductive control, confidential termination, paternity and record disclosure need explicit homes.

- [pbu13-0087](#quote-pbu13-0087) — Teen describes discovering pregnancy after leaving a violent partner.
- [pbu13-0088](#quote-pbu13-0088) — Supportive father offers practical means to keep baby amid hostile reactions from others.
- [pbu25-0009](#quote-pbu25-0009) — Old insurance data exposed prenatal care to estranged family.
- [pbu25-0010](#quote-pbu25-0010) — Hospital complaints displace the pregnancy experience she wanted.
- [pbu25-0155](#quote-pbu25-0155) — Assault survivor fears paternity uncertainty and costly testing wait.
- [pbu25-0228](#quote-pbu25-0228) — Teen parent asks whether she can choose to give birth without her mother.
- [pbu25-0297](#quote-pbu25-0297) — Health costs and fear of family seeing records constrain termination access.
- [pbu25-0343](#quote-pbu25-0343) — Teen parent wants announcement without public due date and local gossip.

### Pets, elder care, emergency kinship care and disabled family members change the preparation problem.

The existing older-child/toddler section is too narrow for these obligations. Fear of a biting dog is not adequately represented by the stock accusation of pregnancy hormones.

- [pbu13-0012](#quote-pbu13-0012) — Elder care and moving exhaust both partners before their first baby.
- [pbu13-0106](#quote-pbu13-0106) — Emergency care assumes someone else can mind older child, but partner is also her carer.
- [pbu13-0107](#quote-pbu13-0107) — Estrangement after abuse makes assumed family help and easy trust unavailable.
- [pbu13-0345](#quote-pbu13-0345) — Pregnancy overlaps unexpected custody of nephew and pressure to afford larger home.
- [pbu13-0346](#quote-pbu13-0346) — Temporary kinship care becomes ongoing, with fears about future baby's safety.
- [pbu13-0364](#quote-pbu13-0364) — Dog lands on abdomen while minding disabled siblings two hours from OB, limiting ability to seek care.
- [pbu23-0003](#quote-pbu23-0003) — Father refuses help while expecting emotional care from his pregnant daughter.
- [pbu23-0018](#quote-pbu23-0018) — Partner's illness and previous suicide attempt interact with her heightened pregnancy anxiety.
- [pbu23-0067](#quote-pbu23-0067) — Pregnancy-time injury limits the partner's ability to lift or care for their baby.
- [pbu23-0071](#quote-pbu23-0071) — Father's stated inability to be alone with the baby complicates newborn coparenting.
- [pbu23-0072](#quote-pbu23-0072) — Worries that dependence on relatives makes proposed early overnight care unpredictable.
- [pbu23-0339](#quote-pbu23-0339) — Dog jumping onto abdomen begins fear that escalating behaviour will endanger pregnancy and newborn.
- [pbu23-0340](#quote-pbu23-0340) — Reassuring explanations made her doubt pet concerns; replies support proposing a different home for the dog.
- [pbu25-0012](#quote-pbu25-0012) — Dog behavior and fall risk compound pregnancy exhaustion.
- [pbu25-0113](#quote-pbu25-0113) — Elder care logistics displace birth and breastfeeding preparation.
- [pbu25-0193](#quote-pbu25-0193) — Dog care feels physically risky after multiple losses.
- [pbu25-0244](#quote-pbu25-0244) — Wants emotional preparation and action on a dog risk, beyond logistics.

### Community rules, public anger, humor and cultural beliefs are evidence too.

A moderation policy describes where peer support stops; unaffordable care prompts identification with Luigi; a pregnancy-as-software-release joke, a Chinese prediction question and fear about attending a funeral do not support a preparation-product demand claim. Keep these small subjects rather than inventing a large pattern.

- [pbu13-0019](#quote-pbu13-0019) — Unexpected pregnancy bills provoke identification with a public act of violence.
- [pbu13-0020](#quote-pbu13-0020) — Connects unaffordable care with desperation and ignoring symptoms.
- [pbu13-0103](#quote-pbu13-0103) — Moderator explains deleting symptom posts to avoid inaccurate reassurance or alarm.
- [pbu13-0104](#quote-pbu13-0104) — Moderator describes waiting at home and differences in provider empathy; claim unverified.
- [pbu13-0165](#quote-pbu13-0165) — After his own losses, argues terminology should distinguish early miscarriage from losing a child.
- [pbu13-0382](#quote-pbu13-0382) — Asks whether attending funeral during pregnancy is unsafe or bad luck.
- [pbu23-0195](#quote-pbu23-0195) — Humorous software-release analogy describes late-pregnancy discomfort and unstable newborn sleep.
- [pbu23-0272](#quote-pbu23-0272) — Asks about a sex-prediction website while acknowledging chance and citing three past pregnancies.

### Original-language evidence and commercial-testimonial context remain visible.

The Spanish account links paternity-related non-support, all pregnancy costs, suicidal thoughts, an induction wish and fear of custody consequences from disclosure. The Tagalog/English passage compares bump/abdominal sensations across pregnancies. AZO and Nampons testimonials carry possible-promotion context in the ur13 ledger; neither is independent proof of efficacy.

- [pbu13-0024](#quote-pbu13-0024) — Persistent genital odor creates fear of examinations despite frequent washing.
- [pbu13-0025](#quote-pbu13-0025) — AZO testimonial; possible seeded promotion, unverified author-attributed outcome.
- [pbu13-0279](#quote-pbu13-0279) — At 31 weeks, partner refuses support pending post-birth paternity test; quote remains in Spanish.
- [pbu13-0280](#quote-pbu13-0280) — Wants induction amid depression and suicidal thoughts, fears seeking care will cost custody; Spanish original.
- [pbu13-0297](#quote-pbu13-0297) — Nampons testimonial links pregnancy nosebleeds with convenient clotting product; possible promotion, outcome unverified.
- [pbu23-0157](#quote-pbu23-0157) — Compares left-sided abdominal firmness and bump visibility with a previous thinner pregnancy; reassurance does not settle curiosity.

The subjects above need explicit homes in the rewritten findings rather than a return to the historical exclusion buckets. The peer-moderation policy is an advisor/community statement, the support appeals include relatives and partners, and commercial testimonials retain their provenance caveats; they are not all parent product requests.

## Money

The stated currency amounts and cost ranges are below, followed by the money/purchase/resource index. Relative income, rent burden and unpriced transactions are also retained. The index also retains unspecified costs, purchases without a price, gifts, supplies used, paid-help arrangements and the access constraints surrounding them. Owning or trying a product does not prove it was bought; a contemplated cost, a promised contribution or projected lost income is not a paid price. Full fields are linked so the amounts and every associated item remain reviewable.

| ID | Amount / transaction | Status and qualification |
|---|---|---|
| [pbu13-0005](#quote-pbu13-0005) | $80k daycare over four years; a new car and rearranged sleeping space | Projected consequences of a third child, not amounts already paid. |
| [pbu13-0058](#quote-pbu13-0058) | $800 lost by her mother if partner moves out | Prospective household/rent loss after a breakup; no abortion price. |

| [pbu25-0306](#quote-pbu25-0306) | About 1100 dollars for two floor-seat concert tickets | Bought before learning of pregnancy; the conflict is over attending, not a birth preparation purchase. |

### Unpriced purchases, care, refunds, gifts, income and access — full index

No completed monetary refund or refund amount is reported in this share. Nonrefundable travel is reported; free items or scans and a promised contribution are recorded as such. The AZO/Nampons passages are testimonials with possible-promotion context in the ledger, not verified buying or efficacy evidence.

**Reading against §8 and §10:** Payment for a private scan, a home cuff, screening, paternity testing, vitamins, books, childcare, therapy or physical care establishes only that transaction or consideration. Financial security can coexist with ambivalence, and extreme money constraints can determine continuation, access or leaving a partner. The “inexpensive beside care” comparison is insufficient when the parent cannot afford deposits, scans, formula, lost wages or safe housing. No amount here prices the proposed guide.

## Requests

This index separates outright product/plan/list/service inquiries from general reassurance and indirect format signals. It records the request without treating it as willingness to pay. Most requests are specific to a current body, history or access problem; none names the proposed Mamivio guide. Each linked id has its complete verbatim field above.

### Products, recipes and practical supplies

Outright product, ingredient, recipe or equipment requests; a recommendation request is not a purchase commitment.

- [pbu13-0105](#quote-pbu13-0105) — Excitement and mixed feelings after loss accompany an ordinary request for pregnancy recipes.

### Additional explicit information-format requests (not purchase intent)

These ask for particular kinds of information or an interface change. The risk-statistics, story and labeling requests do not amount to a request to buy a general pregnancy guide.

- [pbu13-0119](#quote-pbu13-0119) — Even an advice worker values someone breaking choices into steps.
- [pbu13-0286](#quote-pbu13-0286) — Wants even one success story to endure two-week wait after slow HCG rise.
- [pbu13-0414](#quote-pbu13-0414) — Partner asks for possibilities rather than false reassurance after severe lung findings.
- [pbu13-0415](#quote-pbu13-0415) — Explicitly welcomes difficult outcomes to inform decisions, not only positive stories.
- [pbu23-0027](#quote-pbu23-0027) — Quiet symptoms and spotting provoke anxiety before the first scan.
- [pbu23-0036](#quote-pbu23-0036) — Week searches show miscarriage previews; she switches search engines and leaves Facebook.
- [pbu25-0048](#quote-pbu25-0048) — Requests actual recovery experiences rather than generic reassurance.
- [pbu25-0265](#quote-pbu25-0265) — Unexplained postbirth procedure leaves trauma and a difficult search for support.
- [pbu25-0367](#quote-pbu25-0367) — Explicitly requests good and bad symptom outcomes rather than reassurance alone.
- [pbu25-0389](#quote-pbu25-0389) — Expensive fertility vitamin subscription continued after conception prompts fear and labeling complaint.

**Limits on interpreting requests:** Describing a treatment, already having a therapist/doula, a relative recommending a service, asking whether a symptom is normal, and requesting a positive story are not by themselves requests to buy a guide. The step-by-step appreciation and labeling complaint are desired-format signals; the “both good and bad” requests explicitly reject reassurance-only curation. Keep those distinctions in §6 and the §10 product verdict.
